10. Special Evolution Requirements

Not every Entheon evolves through ordinary level progression. Some species require additional conditions that reflect their established concepts.

10.1 Bond Evolution

Sairi → Sairune

Requirement:

Level 30 + High Bond Sairi must reach the required level while having developed a sufficiently strong bond with its trainer. Meeting the level requirement without sufficient Bond does not trigger evolution.

10.2 Item Evolution

Tivvi → Tivarra

Requirement:

Storm Shard The Storm Shard is a special evolutionary item that resonates with Tivvi’s Volt affinity. The item triggers the evolution when used appropriately.

10.3 Time-Based Evolution

Esvin → Esvar

Requirement:

Level 30 + Nighttime Esvin must reach the required level during the appropriate nighttime condition.

10.4 Bond + Level Evolution

Yori → Yorvale

Requirement:

Level 40 + High Bond Yori must reach Level 40 and have developed a sufficiently strong bond with its trainer.

10.5 Artifact Evolution

Orli → Orlith

Requirement:

Level 30 + Ancient Relic Orli requires an Ancient Relic in addition to reaching the required level. The Ancient Relic resonates with Orli’s artifact-like nature and enables its evolution.

10.6 Environmental Milestone Evolution

Rilsa → Rilsaryn

Requirement:

Level 32 + Radiant Garden exposure Rilsa must reach the required level and experience a meaningful exposure to a suitable Radiant Garden environment. The environmental requirement represents a developmental milestone rather than a simple location check.

11. Branching Evolution

Veyli Evolution

Veyli has three possible evolutionary forms: > Veyli → Veylith > Veyli → Veyrune > Veyli → Veyvara The evolution path is determined by the evolutionary catalyst used.

Veyli → Veylith

Requirement:

Level 28 + Emberstone

Veyli → Veyrune

Requirement:

Level 28 + Tide Pearl

Veyli → Veyvara

Requirement:

Level 28 + Mystic Crystal Veyli does not automatically evolve into one of the three forms merely by reaching Level 28. The trainer’s chosen catalyst determines the resulting evolutionary branch. Once evolution occurs, the resulting form is fixed.

12. Evolutionary Forms Are Fixed

Each species has a predetermined evolutionary relationship. The system never introduces an alternative evolutionary form unless that form has been explicitly established in the species database. Evolution therefore changes the Entheon’s species identity, rather than simply applying a generic upgrade.

13. Entheon That Do Not Evolve

Single-stage Entheon do not have an evolution requirement and cannot evolve. Their Level can still increase normally. Level progression can provide: - New moves. - Combat development. - Other explicitly defined species progression. The following are permanently single-stage: - All established one-stage Entheon. - All established Legendary Entheon. Legendaries are therefore not expected to evolve.

14. Delayed Evolution

A trainer may deliberately delay an available evolution. An Entheon that has declined evolution: - Remains in its current form. - Continues gaining XP normally. - Continues learning moves normally. - Retains its accumulated XP and Level. - May evolve later when the trainer chooses to proceed. If an Entheon has multiple possible evolutionary branches, the trainer’s choice of catalyst determines the branch when evolution is eventually performed.

15. Evolution and Learned Moves

  1. Update the Entheon’s species.
  2. Preserve its existing Level.
  3. Preserve its accumulated XP.
  4. Preserve its Nature.
  5. Preserve its Personality and established Individual Quirks unless the story naturally develops them.
  6. Preserve an existing Special Move/Ability.
  7. Check the new species’ Level-Up Learnset.
  8. Add any newly available moves appropriate to the Entheon’s current level.
  9. Update the Entheon’s evolution state. Evolution does not reset the Entheon’s Level or XP.

16. Special Move/Ability and Evolution

Special Moves/Abilities are separate from ordinary moves. If an individual Entheon already possesses a Special Move/Ability before evolving: > The Special Move/Ability is retained through evolution unless its established description explicitly states otherwise. Evolution does not automatically grant a Special Move/Ability to an individual that did not previously possess one. Therefore: > Individual A evolves while possessing a Special Ability → retains it. > Individual B evolves without a Special Ability → does not automatically gain one merely because it evolved. This preserves individual variation within the same species.

Example

Nimblet has reached Level 16. Evolution requirement satisfied: Nimblet → Nymbril “Nimblet is ready to evolve into Nymbril. Would you like to proceed?” If the player chooses No, Nimblet remains Nimblet. If the player later chooses Yes, the evolution proceeds.

18. Design Principle

Evolution should feel like a meaningful development milestone rather than an automatic statistical upgrade. The system therefore combines: - Level - Experience - Training - Bond - Items - Time - Environment - Player choice Different Entheon can therefore have different evolutionary journeys while remaining completely deterministic within their established species rules.

Item System

1. Purpose

The Item System defines the physical items that exist within the creature world. Items are part of the world’s established setting and may be referenced by the creature encyclopedia, story events, trainers, shops, laboratories, researchers and other world elements. This document defines what items exist and what they do.

2. Item Categories

The world uses the following major item categories: 1. Capture Devices 2. Evolution Items 3. Healing and Recovery Items 4. Status Recovery Items 5. Battle Items 6. Training Items 7. Held Items 8. Key Items and Special Artifacts Not every category needs hundreds of individual items. Items should have clear, distinct purposes so that the world remains understandable and manageable.

3. Capture Devices

Capture devices are small crystalline devices used to contain and transport creatures. They replace the traditional “Capture Crystal” concept while functioning similarly at a mechanical level. A Capture Crystal temporarily converts a creature into a contained energy state and stores that creature within the crystal. When released, the creature returns to its physical form.

3.1 Canonical Physical Design

All Capture Crystals share the same fundamental physical design. A Capture Crystal is a slender, elongated, faceted crystal, resembling a polished gemstone shard with a distinctive tapered silhouette. The basic silhouette remains consistent across all grades of Capture Crystal. Higher-grade crystals are not fundamentally different objects; they are smaller, more refined versions of the same design. The crystal itself is the recognizable visual identity of the capture system.

Size by Grade

Device Approximate Crystal Size Typical Configuration
Capture Crystal Approximately the length of an adult male middle finger Necklace
Refined Crystal Noticeably smaller than a standard Capture Crystal Necklace, bracelet or compact mount
Primal Crystal Small enough for discreet personal mounting Bracelet, necklace or equipment mount
Sovereign Crystal Approximately one-third the length of a standard Capture Crystal Bracelet, pendant or specialized mount

These dimensions are approximate rather than exact measurements. The important distinction is that higher-grade Capture Crystals are progressively smaller while retaining the same fundamental shape.

3.2 Mounting and Wearing

The standard and most common way to carry a Capture Crystal is as a necklace or pendant. A standard Capture Crystal is relatively large, making it impractical or cumbersome for most bracelet configurations. Smaller, higher-grade crystals allow for greater freedom in how they are carried. Common configurations include: - Necklace or pendant. - Bracelet-mounted crystal. - Belt-mounted crystal. - Equipment-mounted crystal. - Specialized custom mounting. The mounting itself does not affect capture performance. The choice of mounting is primarily determined by: - Personal preference. - Comfort. - Fashion. - Profession. - Equipment requirements. - Available craftsmanship. - Crystal size. A trainer who specifically wants a bracelet-mounted Capture Crystal will generally need to invest in a smaller, higher-grade crystal or a specialized mounting solution. The necklace configuration remains the ordinary and most widespread form.

3.3 Visual Consistency

Despite differences in size, craftsmanship and mounting, all Capture Crystals should remain immediately recognizable as the same type of device. Manufacturers and individuals may vary: - The exact crystal cut. - Facet arrangement. - Mounting hardware. - Decorative elements. - Chain, cord or bracelet design. - Minor casing details. - Subtle color accents. However, the underlying slender, elongated, faceted crystal silhouette must remain consistent. The device should never resemble a spherical capsule or another unrelated containment device.

4. Capture Device Grades

4.1 Capture Crystal

Purpose: Standard capture device. The basic and most commonly available capture device. A standard Capture Crystal is approximately the length of an adult male middle finger and is most commonly worn as a necklace or pendant. Suitable for ordinary wild creatures and routine trainer use.

4.2 Refined Crystal

Purpose: Improved capture device. A more sophisticated and smaller version of the standard Capture Crystal, featuring a stronger containment field and more refined crystal construction. It provides a higher capture probability than a standard Capture Crystal, particularly against stronger or more resistant creatures. Its reduced size makes bracelet-mounted and other compact configurations more practical.

4.3 Primal Crystal

Purpose: High-grade capture device. A powerful and comparatively expensive Capture Crystal designed for difficult captures. Its highly refined crystal structure allows it to maintain a stronger containment field while remaining small enough for discreet personal mounting. Primal Crystals are uncommon compared with ordinary Capture Crystals.

4.4 Sovereign Crystal

Purpose: Exceptional capture device. An extremely rare and highly refined Capture Crystal. A Sovereign Crystal is approximately one-third the length of a standard Capture Crystal while retaining the same fundamental elongated, faceted design. Its compact size makes it particularly suitable for bracelet-mounted, pendant or other specialized configurations. A Sovereign Crystal is capable of attempting containment against creatures that would normally resist lesser devices. Sovereign Crystals are not ordinary equipment. Their rarity and cost make them unsuitable for routine use. A Sovereign Crystal does not guarantee successful capture.

5. Capture Rules

Capture devices do not automatically make a captured creature obedient. Successful capture establishes a containment and ownership relationship, but the creature’s: - Nature - Personality - Trust - Bond - Previous experiences - Individual temperament still influence its relationship with its trainer. A captured creature may therefore be: - Cooperative. - Cautious. - Distrustful. - Excited. - Resistant. - Deeply loyal. Capture is the beginning of a trainer-creature relationship, not a replacement for that relationship.

6. Capture Difficulty

Capture probability can be influenced by: - The creature’s current condition. - Its Level. - Its relative strength. - Its rarity. - Its temperament. - Whether it is willing to be captured. - The quality of the capture device. - Special circumstances surrounding the encounter. A weakened creature is generally easier to contain than a healthy creature at full strength. Extremely powerful or exceptionally rare creatures may resist even high-grade capture devices. Legendary creatures are not automatically capturable merely because a sufficiently powerful device exists.

7. Evolution Items

Evolution items are specialized objects that can trigger or enable specific evolutionary transformations. They are not generic power-up items. Each evolution item has a defined purpose and should only trigger an evolution when the appropriate species and conditions are present.

7.1 Storm Shard

Associated Evolution: Tivvi → Tivarra

Requirement:

Storm Shard The Storm Shard is a crystalline fragment charged with concentrated Volt energy. When used on an eligible Tivvi, it resonates with Tivvi’s natural Volt affinity and enables its evolution into Tivarra.

7.2 Emberstone

Associated Evolution: Veyli → Veylith

Requirement:

Level 28 + Emberstone An Emberstone is a warm, fire-charged evolutionary stone. It resonates with Flame-affinity energy and directs Veyli’s branching evolution toward Veylith.

7.3 Tide Pearl

Associated Evolution: Veyli → Veyrune

Requirement:

Level 28 + Tide Pearl A Tide Pearl is a naturally formed pearl containing concentrated Tide energy. It directs Veyli’s branching evolution toward Veyrune.

7.4 Mystic Crystal

Associated Evolution: Veyli → Veyvara

Requirement:

Level 28 + Mystic Crystal A Mystic Crystal contains concentrated Mystic energy. It directs Veyli’s branching evolution toward Veyvara.

7.5 Ancient Relic

Associated Evolution: Orli → Orlith

Requirement:

Level 30 + Ancient Relic The Ancient Relic is an old artifact containing residual energy from a previous age. Its resonance is specifically compatible with Orli’s artifact-like nature and enables its evolution into Orlith.

8. Evolution Items Are Species-Specific

Evolution items do not function as universal evolution triggers. For example: > An Emberstone does not cause every Flame-affinity creature to evolve. The species database determines which Entheon, if any, can evolve through a particular item. The item system never introduces an additional species-item relationship unless it has been explicitly established.

9. Healing and Recovery Items

Healing items restore a creature’s physical condition. The item system uses a small number of clearly differentiated recovery items rather than dozens of nearly identical products.

9.1 Basic Restore

A basic restorative item used to treat minor injuries and restore a limited amount of physical stamina. Suitable for routine field use.

9.2 Greater Restore

A stronger restorative item capable of treating more serious injuries and restoring significantly more stamina. More expensive than Basic Restore.

9.3 Full Restore

A high-grade restorative capable of restoring a creature to excellent physical condition after severe injury. Expensive and generally reserved for important situations.

10. Status Recovery Items

Status conditions can be treated using specialized restorative items.

10.1 Purity Tonic

A general-purpose treatment for common harmful status conditions.

10.2 Cleansing Elixir

A stronger treatment capable of removing more persistent or severe status effects.

10.3 Revitalizing Elixir

A specialized restorative used when a creature is exhausted or suffering from a debilitating condition. These items are intended as a separate category from ordinary healing.

11. Battle Items

Battle items temporarily influence combat performance. They may improve attributes such as: - Attack - Defense - Speed - Accuracy - Resistance - Affinity output Battle items provide temporary effects and do not permanently increase a creature’s underlying progression. They should be used sparingly during ordinary battles.

12. Training Items

Training items assist with development outside ordinary combat. Examples include: - Resistance-training equipment. - Affinity-control focuses. - Targeting devices. - Practice constructs. - Specialized training aids. Training equipment can make training more effective but does not bypass the ordinary progression system.

13. Held Items

Some items can be carried or worn by a creature and provide a persistent passive effect while equipped. Held items should be relatively uncommon and meaningful. Examples may include:

Item General Effect
Affinity Charm Slightly improves control of the user’s primary Affinity.
Vitality Band Improves stamina during prolonged activity.
Focus Lens Improves precision when using techniques requiring concentration.
Guard Charm Provides a minor defensive benefit.
Swift Band Provides a minor mobility benefit.

These are examples of the intended category and may be expanded later. Held items do not automatically replace a creature’s Special Move/Ability.

14. Key Items and Special Artifacts

Key Items are unique or limited objects associated with important locations, events, quests or creatures. Unlike ordinary consumables, they may exist primarily to: - Unlock locations. - Enable special interactions. - Trigger evolution. - Reveal information. - Resolve story situations. - Interact with ancient technology. - Interact with Affinity phenomena. Key Items should generally have a specific narrative purpose.

15. Bond Crystal

The Bond Crystal is a special item associated with the relationship between a trainer and their creature. It is not a capture device. A Bond Crystal is a personal crystal that represents the relationship between trainer and creature. It may be worn as: - A necklace. - A bracelet. - A belt-mounted crystal. - Another personal carrying arrangement. The Bond Crystal reacts to the emotional connection between trainer and creature. Its appearance and resonance may subtly change as the relationship develops. The Bond Crystal does not replace capture devices and cannot be used to capture creatures.

16. Item Rarity

Items may have different availability levels.

Common

Widely available everyday items. Examples: - Capture Crystals. - Basic healing items. - Basic training equipment.

Uncommon

More expensive or specialized items. Examples: - Refined Crystals. - Greater Restores. - Specialized training equipment.

Rare

Items that require significant resources, specialized knowledge or fortunate circumstances. Examples: - Primal Crystals. - Evolution items. - Advanced restorative items.

Extremely Rare

Items that are difficult to obtain and may be associated with major events. Examples: - Sovereign Crystals. - Unique ancient artifacts.

Unique

One-of-a-kind or extremely limited story objects. These should only exist when specifically established.

17. Item Acquisition

Items can be obtained through ordinary world activities such as: - Purchasing them from shops. - Receiving them from researchers or organizations. - Finding them in the wild. - Discovering them in ruins. - Receiving them as rewards. - Crafting them where appropriate. - Completing important tasks. - Trading with other people. Rare and unique items should generally require a meaningful reason or opportunity to obtain them.

18. Item Economy

Kaleo has an understandable item economy. Ordinary trainers should have reasonable access to: - Basic Capture Crystals. - Basic healing items. - Common training equipment. High-grade capture devices, evolution items and rare artifacts should be substantially more difficult to obtain. The existence of an item in the database does not mean every trainer automatically possesses one.

19. Item Naming Principle

The setting should use its own terminology. The following terms should not be used as canonical world terminology: - Poké Ball - Great Ball - Ultra Ball - Master Ball - Pokémon Center - Pokédex Kaleo uses setting-specific terminology for these concepts.

Canonical creature term:** **Entheon

Singular: Entheon   Plural: Entheon   Individual: an Entheon   Species: Entheon species The current canonical capture-device terminology is: > Capture Crystal → Refined Crystal → Primal Crystal → Sovereign Crystal The creature species themselves use Entheon as the established canonical term throughout the setting.

20. Expansion Rules

Additional items may be added to the database later. New items should: 1. Have a clearly defined purpose. 2. Belong to an established item category or justify a new category. 3. Have a distinct mechanical or narrative function. 4. Avoid duplicating an existing item’s role without a meaningful reason. 5. Be compatible with the established creature, Affinity, evolution and progression systems. 6. Not create new evolution paths unless those paths are explicitly added to the species database. The item database is intended to remain coherent and manageable rather than becoming an exhaustive catalogue of minor variations.

21. Current Evolution-Item Reference

Item Associated Creature Evolution
Storm Shard Tivvi Tivarra
Emberstone Veyli Veylith
Tide Pearl Veyli Veyrune
Mystic Crystal Veyli Veyvara
Ancient Relic Orli Orlith

Additional evolution items can be added when new evolution mechanics are deliberately established.

Trainer, Capture & Battle Systems

Trainer Status

Anyone can become a Trainer. There is no special aptitude, bloodline, profession, or other inherent requirement needed to become one. Being a Trainer is a normal and common part of society. Most people have at least one Entheon, although being a Trainer does not necessarily mean that someone is a competitive or professional battler. A Trainer may have Entheon for companionship, work, recreation, battling, research, protection, transportation, or other practical purposes.

Age Requirement

The minimum age for independent Trainer status is 15. At age 15, a person may:

  • Register as a Trainer.
  • Receive a TraID.
  • Legally own Entheon.
  • Purchase and register Capture Crystals.
  • Capture Entheon independently.
  • Participate in official Trainer battles.
  • Challenge Gyms.
  • Enter tournaments and other Trainer competitions.

People under 15 may still live with Entheon, care for them, train alongside them, and form bonds with them under the responsibility of a parent, guardian, or other legally responsible adult. There is no separate junior TraID or junior Trainer licensing system.

15 is the age of full independent Trainer status.

Trainer Registration

Anyone who captures their first Entheon must legally register as a Trainer. Registration is completed through an authorized Trainer registration facility. Upon registration, the Trainer receives a TraID, which serves as their official Trainer identification. Registration is straightforward and widely accessible. There is no restrictive licensing process or test determining whether someone is considered worthy of becoming a Trainer.

Capture Crystal Registration

Capture Crystals are individually identifiable and are linked to their legal owner. When a Trainer purchases a new Capture Crystal, they present their TraID. The crystal is then automatically registered and linked to that Trainer, even while it remains empty. When an Entheon is captured into a registered Capture Crystal, the crystal automatically identifies the Entheon’s unique biological signature. The Entheon is then associated with that Capture Crystal and the Trainer’s TraID. No separate ownership filing is required for each individual capture. The basic process is:

Purchase Crystal → Present TraID → Crystal linked to Trainer → Capture Entheon → Biological signature recognized → Entheon registered to Trainer

This registration system also provides the foundation for ownership transfers, trading, stolen Entheon, and illegal captures.

Battle Culture

Entheon battles are a common and socially accepted part of everyday life. Battles occur in many different contexts, including:

  • Roadside Trainer battles.
  • Friendly and practice battles.
  • Gym challenges.
  • Local events.
  • Tournaments.
  • Professional competitions.
  • Occupational and practical training.

Battling is not restricted to professional Trainers. Ordinary Trainers may battle for recreation, practice, social interaction, reputation, or income.

Roadside Trainer Battles

Registered Trainers may challenge one another while travelling. Roadside battles can provide legitimate income, making travelling and battling a viable lifestyle for some Trainers. Depending on the circumstances, battles may involve agreed rewards such as money, items, services, or other forms of compensation. A Trainer is not required to accept every challenge. Declining a battle is not inherently suspicious or dishonorable. Because Entheon ownership and battling are so common, encountering Trainers battling in appropriate public areas is a normal part of everyday life.

Battle Safety

A fundamental rule of Entheon combat is:

An Entheon cannot kill another Entheon through direct combat.

Entheon battles can still result in:

  • A knockout.
  • Exhaustion.
  • Temporary injuries.
  • Serious but non-lethal injuries.
  • The need for medical treatment or recovery.

However, direct Entheon-on-Entheon combat cannot result in the death of an Entheon. This is an inherent rule of Entheon combat rather than merely a regulation imposed by competitive organizations. This does not mean that Entheon are immortal. Entheon can still die from natural causes, illness, accidents, environmental hazards, or other circumstances unrelated to direct Entheon combat. The rule also reinforces the individual agency of Entheon. A Trainer cannot simply command an Entheon to kill another Entheon.

Gyms

There are seven recognized Gyms. Each Gym:

  • Specializes in a fixed Affinity.
  • Has a designated Gym Leader.
  • Can be challenged in any order.
  • Has an informal recommended progression based on expected difficulty.
  • Does not require previous Gyms to be completed before it can be challenged.

The recommended order is guidance rather than a mandatory progression system. A sufficiently experienced Trainer may challenge a later or more difficult Gym without first completing the others.

Gym Crests

Defeating a Gym Leader awards the Trainer that Gym’s Crest. There are seven Gym Crests corresponding to the seven recognized Gyms. Crests are physical achievements and insignia representing a Trainer’s successful Gym challenges. They may be collected in any order. The exact significance of possessing all seven Gym Crests, including what competitions or opportunities they qualify a Trainer for, will be established as part of the wider League and tournament structure.

Professional and Competitive Battling

Battling exists across a broad spectrum. A Trainer may battle casually with friends, challenge other Trainers while travelling, participate in local events, pursue Gym challenges, or eventually compete at a professional level. Professional battling can therefore become a legitimate career, although the exact structure of professional competition, rankings, sponsorships, tournaments, and leagues is defined separately.

Rogue Trainers

Because Trainer registration is legally required after capturing an Entheon, unregistered Trainers can exist outside the established system. A rogue Trainer is an individual who possesses or trains captured Entheon without maintaining the legally required Trainer registration. Being a rogue Trainer does not automatically mean that the individual is a criminal. However, registration is straightforward, accessible, and extremely common, meaning that most people who deliberately remain unregistered are expected to have a reason for avoiding the legal system. Rogue Trainers may be associated with activities such as:

  • Stolen Entheon.
  • Illegal Entheon trafficking.
  • Underground battling.
  • Concealing their identity.
  • Criminal organizations.
  • Deliberately avoiding legal restrictions or oversight.

Rogue Trainer is primarily a common social term rather than necessarily a formal legal classification. The exact enforcement mechanisms and legal consequences surrounding rogue Trainers will be established alongside the broader rules for Entheon ownership, capture, transfer, and theft.

3. Entheon Ownership & Capture

Legal Ownership

An Entheon becomes legally associated with a Trainer when it is successfully captured into a Capture Crystal registered to that Trainer. Capture therefore establishes both the physical containment of the Entheon and its legal ownership record. The Capture Crystal’s registration system automatically links the captured Entheon to the Trainer’s TraID.

Capturing Wild Entheon

Wild Entheon can be captured using Capture Crystals. A successful capture places the Entheon within the Capture Crystal and registers its unique biological signature to the crystal and its Trainer. Capture does not automatically determine an Entheon’s personality, Nature, or Bond with its Trainer. An Entheon remains an individual being with its own personality, preferences, memories, and agency after capture. A newly captured Entheon may therefore require time and interaction to become comfortable with its new Trainer.

Capture Crystal Habitats

A Capture Crystal is more than a container. When an Entheon is captured, the crystal creates and maintains a self-contained miniature habitat suited to that individual Entheon’s needs. The habitat automatically adapts to the Entheon’s biological and environmental requirements. An Entheon’s habitat is influenced by factors such as:

  • Species.
  • Affinity.
  • Biological requirements.
  • Environmental preferences.
  • Individual characteristics.

Affinity alone does not determine the habitat. Two Entheon with the same Affinity may therefore have completely different environments within their Capture Crystals. For example, one Fire-affinity Entheon may thrive in a volcanic environment containing heated rock and magma, while another Fire-affinity Entheon may prefer a warm, densely vegetated forest environment. The Capture Crystal provides a safe, self-sustaining environment in which the Entheon can rest, sleep, eat, and otherwise live comfortably while contained.

Habitat Adaptation

A Capture Crystal’s internal habitat can change as its Entheon changes. Evolution, changes in Affinity, maturation, or changing biological requirements may cause the habitat to adapt accordingly. The habitat therefore represents the individual Entheon, rather than simply providing a generic environment based on its species or Affinity.

Capture Does Not Equal Obedience

Capturing an Entheon establishes legal ownership and provides a safe habitat, but it does not erase the Entheon’s individuality or automatically create complete obedience. An Entheon retains:

  • Its Nature.
  • Its Personality.
  • Its memories.
  • Its preferences.
  • Its individual agency.
  • Its existing relationships and experiences.

The quality of the relationship between Trainer and Entheon is instead influenced by their interactions and Bond. A newly captured Entheon may therefore be cooperative, uncertain, resistant, enthusiastic, frightened, curious, or otherwise behave according to its individual Personality and circumstances.

Capture establishes ownership. It does not manufacture loyalty.

Transfer of Ownership

Entheon can be legally transferred between Trainers through mechanisms such as trading or gifting. When an Entheon is transferred, its registration is updated to reflect the new Trainer and Capture Crystal ownership. A transfer does not erase the Entheon’s:

  • Memories.
  • Nature.
  • Personality.
  • Previous experiences.
  • Existing Bond history.

Legal ownership and emotional attachment are therefore separate concepts. An Entheon may legally belong to a new Trainer while still remembering and caring about its previous Trainer.

Stolen Entheon

Taking another Trainer’s Entheon or Capture Crystal without authorization is illegal. Because Capture Crystals are individually registered, simply taking a crystal does not automatically transfer ownership. The existing registration remains associated with the original Trainer until a legitimate ownership transfer is completed. The exact legal procedures surrounding stolen Entheon, ownership disputes, illegal captures, and rogue Trainers will be established as part of the wider ownership and enforcement system.

4. Entheon Storage

Active Entheon Limit

A Trainer may have up to six Entheon in their active group at any given time. Six is the maximum number of Entheon that a Trainer normally carries with them while travelling, battling, or participating in activities. There is no limit to the total number of Entheon a Trainer may legally own. A Trainer may therefore capture and own additional Entheon beyond their six active Entheon.

Automatic Storage

When a Trainer captures an Entheon while already having six active Entheon, the newly captured Entheon is automatically transferred to the Trainer’s registered remote storage. The Trainer does not lose ownership of the Entheon. It simply becomes part of their stored collection rather than their active group. The basic process is:

Fewer than six active Entheon → newly captured Entheon joins the active group.

Six active Entheon → newly captured Entheon is automatically transferred to registered storage.

A Trainer may later exchange an active Entheon with one held in storage. Storage transfers automatically update the Trainer’s registered Entheon roster.

Remote Storage

Entheon that are not part of a Trainer’s active group are maintained in secure remote storage. Stored Entheon continue to live within their Capture Crystals’ individual habitats and remain legally associated with their Trainer. A Trainer can retrieve an Entheon from storage and exchange it with one of their active Entheon. The exact technical infrastructure used to access and operate the remote storage network will be established as part of the world’s broader technology rules.

Entheon Activity Requirement

Entheon are living beings rather than permanent collectibles. A Trainer is expected to maintain an ongoing relationship with their Entheon, including those held in storage. If a Trainer has no meaningful interaction with an Entheon for more than one month, the storage system flags that Entheon as inactive. The Trainer receives a notification or warning before the inactivity period expires. If the Trainer does not meaningfully interact with the Entheon before the period expires, the Entheon is automatically released into the wild. When this occurs:

  • The Entheon’s ownership registration is terminated.
  • The Entheon is no longer associated with the Trainer’s TraID.
  • The Capture Crystal is returned to the Trainer as an empty, unregistered crystal.
  • The released Entheon becomes independent and is free to establish its own life in the wild.

Meaningful Interaction

Meaningful interaction can include:

  • Taking the Entheon out of storage.
  • Spending time with the Entheon.
  • Training with it.
  • Battling with it.
  • Travelling with it.
  • Caring for it.
  • Personally checking on or interacting with it through an approved storage system.
  • Other direct interaction that demonstrates an ongoing relationship.

Simply owning the Entheon, keeping its Capture Crystal registered, or viewing its record does not reset the inactivity period.

Long-Term Inactive Status

A Trainer may place an Entheon into an approved long-term inactive status when there is a legitimate reason for extended separation. While in this status, the normal one-month inactivity release does not apply. This allows Trainers to maintain ownership of Entheon that temporarily cannot travel with them or receive regular interaction without risking automatic release.

Ownership of an Entheon carries an ongoing responsibility toward that individual.

Active Group & Storage

The six-Entheon active limit is a travel and activity limit, not an ownership limit. A Trainer may therefore maintain a larger collection of Entheon while only travelling with six at a time. This allows Trainers to rotate their active Entheon, develop relationships with different individuals, and select teams appropriate to different situations without treating additional Entheon as disposable possessions.

5. Entheon Battles

General Battle Culture

Entheon battles are a common and socially accepted part of everyday life. Battles can take place in many different contexts, including:

  • Casual battles between friends or acquaintances.
  • Roadside Trainer battles.
  • Gym challenges.
  • Local events and festivals.
  • Exhibition and mock battles.
  • Tournaments.
  • Professional competitions.
  • Training and practical exercises.

The number of Entheon used in an ordinary battle is not universally fixed. Trainers may agree on an appropriate battle format before the battle begins. Common formats can include:

  • 1v1
  • 2v2
  • 3v3
  • 4v4
  • 6v6

Official competitions and Gyms may use their own established formats.

Battle Commands & Entheon Agency

Trainers issue commands to their active Entheon during battle. Commands are instructions rather than absolute control. Entheon retain their individual agency and interpret commands according to their:

  • Nature.
  • Personality.
  • Training.
  • Experience.
  • Bond with their Trainer.
  • Current physical and emotional condition.

An Entheon may therefore refuse, ignore, reinterpret, or otherwise react differently to a command when circumstances warrant it. A strong Trainer is not necessarily one whose Entheon obeys every command without question. Effective battling depends on the ability of a Trainer and their Entheon to understand and coordinate with one another.

Battle Termination

A battle normally ends when:

  • All participating Entheon on one side have been knocked out.
  • A Trainer forfeits the battle.
  • An official or referee stops the battle for safety.
  • Another agreed victory condition is reached.

A knocked-out Entheon cannot continue fighting until it has recovered sufficiently. The exact recovery time depends on the Entheon, the severity of the battle, and the circumstances.

Switching Entheon

Trainers may switch their active Entheon during battle unless a particular battle format or event establishes different rules. Switching allows Trainers to adapt their strategy to changing battle conditions and make use of different Entheon within their active group. An Entheon that is switched out remains part of the battle unless it has been knocked out or otherwise removed from participation.

Move Usage & Stamina

Entheon do not have an artificial limit on how many times a particular move or ability can be used. Instead, repeated use is naturally limited by factors such as:

  • Stamina.
  • Physical strain.
  • Energy expenditure.
  • Concentration.
  • The nature of the move or ability.
  • The Entheon’s physical condition.
  • The surrounding environment where relevant.

A powerful move may therefore be technically usable multiple times, but repeatedly using it can exhaust or otherwise strain the Entheon. Battle performance is consequently influenced by resource management and tactical decision-making, rather than an arbitrary number of permitted uses.

Battle Safety

A fundamental rule of Entheon combat is:

An Entheon cannot kill another Entheon through direct combat.

Entheon battles can still result in:

  • Knockouts.
  • Exhaustion.
  • Temporary injuries.
  • Serious but non-lethal injuries.
  • The need for medical treatment or recovery.

However, direct Entheon-on-Entheon combat cannot result in the death of an Entheon. This is an inherent rule of Entheon combat rather than merely a regulation imposed by competitive organizations. Entheon can still die from natural causes, illness, accidents, environmental hazards, or other circumstances unrelated to direct Entheon combat.

Gym Battles

There are seven recognized Gyms, each specializing in a fixed Affinity. Gym battles use established formats based on the Gym’s recommended difficulty.

Gym Recommended Difficulty Battle Format
Gym 1 Introductory 3v3
Gym 2 Beginner 3v3
Gym 3 Intermediate 3v3
Gym 4 Intermediate/Advanced 4v4
Gym 5 Advanced 4v4
Gym 6 Expert 6v6
Gym 7 Master 6v6

The Gyms may be challenged in any order. The recommended progression represents increasing expected difficulty rather than a mandatory sequence. A Trainer who chooses to challenge a more difficult Gym early must participate in that Gym’s established battle format.

Gym Leader Rosters

Every Gym Leader has a defined roster of six Entheon. The six-member roster represents the Gym Leader’s established team, battle philosophy, and Affinity specialization. The roster remains fixed unless the story or setting establishes that the Gym Leader has changed their team.

Gyms 1–5

For Gyms using fewer than six Entheon, the Gym Leader selects the required number of Entheon from their established six-member roster. Gym battle rosters are selected for an individual challenger based on relevant circumstances, including:

  • The challenger’s experience.
  • The challenger’s demonstrated strategy.
  • The Entheon’s strengths and weaknesses.
  • Battle compatibility.
  • The intended difficulty of the challenge.
  • The Gym Leader’s personality and battle philosophy.

The Gym Leader’s selection is not revealed to the challenger beforehand.

Gyms 6–7

Gyms 6 and 7 use the Gym Leader’s complete six-Entheon roster.

Local Battle Events

Entheon battles are also incorporated into local culture and community celebrations. Towns and cities may feature:

  • Festival battles.
  • Exhibition matches.
  • Mock Trainer battles.
  • Demonstrations.
  • Community tournaments.
  • Charity battles.
  • Seasonal competitions.

These events may take place during celebrations such as lunar festivals, harvest festivals, city anniversaries, or other local traditions. Not every battle at such an event is intended as serious competition. Some are primarily entertainment, allowing spectators to enjoy skilled Trainers and Entheon performing in a festive setting. Entheon battling is therefore naturally integrated into everyday culture rather than existing solely as a professional competitive activity.

Types of Battles

The world contains several broad forms of Entheon battling:

Casual Battles

Informal battles between Trainers for recreation, practice, social interaction, or friendly competition.

Roadside Battles

Trainer challenges encountered while travelling. These may involve agreed rewards or wagers and can provide legitimate income for travelling Trainers.

Gym Battles

Formal challenges against recognized Gym Leaders, using the established format of each Gym and awarding a Gym Crest upon victory.

Local Events

Community-focused battles, exhibitions, demonstrations, and competitions associated with festivals and other local celebrations.

Tournaments

Organized competitive events in which Trainers compete against one another under established rules.

Professional Battles

High-level competitive battling that can form part or all of a Trainer’s career. The exact structure of professional leagues, rankings, sponsorships, and major tournaments is established separately as part of the wider competitive system.

5. Kaleo: Geography, Regions & Travel

Overview

Kaleo is divided into ten distinct regions, each with its own terrain, climate, settlements, wildlife habitats, and local character. The regions are not uniform in size or terrain. A single region may contain several different environments, and the presence of a particular Entheon affinity does not mean every region containing that affinity has the same landscape. Kaleo’s geography is interconnected through Main Trails, Secondary Trails, and established ferry routes, allowing travel between settlements, cities, landmarks, and different regions without requiring a fixed Gym-by-Gym progression.

The Ten Regions

Westmere

Westmere is a temperate region of dense forests, wooded hills, open meadows, and scattered farmland. The forests become particularly dense toward the western and northern portions of the region, while the more settled areas contain fields, small streams, and gently rolling countryside. The region’s most famous natural feature is the Great Tree, an enormous ancient tree standing within a broad clearing surrounded by forest. Westmere is one of Kaleo’s more settled regions, with established Trails connecting its villages and Everhope City, while large stretches of woodland remain relatively wild. Major City: Everhope City Gym: Everhope Gym — Gale Settlements: Valehaven, Briarwood, Oakridge Landmark: The Great Tree

Dunridge

Dunridge is a rugged mountainous region dominated by rocky ridges, steep valleys, cliffs, caves, and narrow mountain passages. Elevation varies considerably, with relatively temperate valleys giving way to harsher terrain at higher elevations. The region forms part of the larger mountain geography separating the western and northern portions of Kaleo. Travelling through Dunridge often means following valleys, crossing rocky passes, or winding around mountain slopes rather than travelling in straight lines. The higher elevations become increasingly cold and eventually transition toward the mountainous environments of Highreach and the colder northern regions. Major City: Stonehaven Gym: None Settlements: Pinewatch, Stonebrook Landmark: None; the mountainous landscape itself defines the region.

Seawick

Seawick is a long, narrow coastal region, stretching considerably farther north-to-south than it does inland. Its geography is dominated by coastline: beaches, rocky shores, coastal cliffs, small bays, sea caves, and scattered coastal wetlands. The interior is comparatively narrow, meaning that many settlements and much of the region’s transportation infrastructure naturally follow the coastline. The region has a strong maritime character. Fishing, shipping, coastal trade, and travel along the Main Trail are important parts of everyday life. Because of its elongated geography, travelling through Seawick can mean following the coast for considerable distances before reaching the next major settlement or regional connection. Major City: Gullhaven Gym: Gullhaven Gym — Tide Settlements: Tidewatch, Driftwood Landmark: None; Seawick’s distinctive coastline defines the region.

Highreach

Highreach is a mountainous northern region characterized by steep peaks, alpine valleys, glaciers, snow-covered slopes, and high-altitude wilderness. The southern portions of the region are comparatively accessible, but the terrain becomes progressively colder and more difficult toward the north. Mountain passes and valleys provide the principal natural travel corridors. Near the northern meeting point of Highreach, Dunridge, and Northvale lies a geothermal area containing the famous Highreach Hot Springs. The springs remain warm despite the surrounding mountainous and snowy environment. Major City: Thermalis Gym: Thermalis Gym — Fire/Stone Settlements: Frostmere, Snowfall Landmark: Highreach Hot Springs

Greenvale

Greenvale is Kaleo’s major agricultural heartland. It consists primarily of broad fertile plains, gently rolling farmland, grasslands, cultivated fields, streams, and small wooded areas. Much of Greenvale has relatively open sightlines, with settlements and farms spread throughout the countryside. The region’s most recognizable structure is the Great Windmill, an enormous functioning agricultural processing facility. Its size makes it visible from considerable distances across the surrounding farmland. Major City: Harveston Gym: Harveston Gym — Verdant Settlements: Millbrook, Meadowfield, Sunvale Landmark: The Great Windmill

Lume

Lume is a relatively small island positioned within the central maritime passage between the major eastern and western portions of Kaleo. The island contains a mixture of coastal terrain, forest, rocky areas, beaches, and undeveloped wilderness. Much of Lume remains natural Entheon habitat rather than being densely developed by humans. Lume Port, the island’s major city, is the primary human population centre and is built around a naturally useful harbor. Lume’s importance comes less from its size and more from its strategic position. Established ferry connections make it an important stopping point for travellers, merchants, and cargo moving between distant parts of Kaleo. Major City: Lume Port Gym: None Settlements: None beyond Lume Port Landmark: Lume Port and the surrounding island environment

Hawthorne

Hawthorne is dominated by the enormous Great Hawthorne Lake, which forms a major geographic feature of the region. The surrounding landscape consists of forests, freshwater wetlands, rolling hills, streams, and fertile areas influenced by the lake. Rivers and waterways branch away from the lake, creating numerous smaller aquatic habitats. Lakecrest City sits directly adjacent to the lake and has developed around the opportunities provided by its freshwater access. The lake also influences travel through the region: Trails naturally follow its shoreline or pass around sections of the water rather than simply cutting directly across it. Major City: Lakecrest City Gym: Lakecrest Gym — Volt Settlements: Stillwater, Brookmere, Riverside Landmark: Great Hawthorne Lake

Northvale

Northvale is a cool northern region of rocky terrain, coniferous forests, hills, river valleys, and increasingly cold landscapes toward its northern edges. One of its defining geographical features is the Cascading River, where water travels through a series of rocky drops, pools, and smaller waterfalls, creating a natural staircase through part of the region. The southern portions of Northvale remain relatively accessible, while the northern terrain becomes colder and more rugged as it approaches the harsher environments around Isen. Northvale therefore acts as an environmental transition between the temperate regions farther south and Kaleo’s genuinely frozen northern wilderness. Major City: Northreach Gym: Northreach Gym — Metal/Frost Settlements: Stonefall, Rivermarch, Frostbridge Landmark: The Cascading River

Eastmere

Eastmere is a broad eastern region with a mixture of coastal plains, forests, grasslands, rolling countryside, rivers, and sheltered bays. The eastern coastline provides numerous natural harbors and coastal environments, while the interior becomes more varied and wooded. Fairhaven is the region’s major urban centre and is connected to the surrounding settlements by established Trails. Eastmere is relatively accessible compared with the mountainous north and west, although its more remote forests and coastal wilderness contain areas that remain largely undeveloped. Major City: Fairhaven Gym: Fairhaven Gym — Mystic Settlements: Seabrook, Windmere Landmark: None; the region’s varied eastern landscape defines it.

Isen

Isen is Kaleo’s most extreme northern environment: a vast frozen tundra characterized by permafrost, snowfields, frozen lakes and rivers, barren plains, ice-covered terrain, and long stretches of wilderness. Vegetation is sparse compared with the southern regions, and permanent human settlement is extremely limited. Winterhold is the region’s major city, with no smaller settlements currently established. Weather and terrain make travel considerably more difficult than in most of Kaleo. Established Trails connect the inhabited areas, but large portions of Isen remain wild and sparsely explored. Isen is not simply a colder version of Highreach: Highreach is mountainous and alpine, while Isen is dominated by expansive frozen tundra. Major City: Winterhold Gym: None Settlements: None Landmark: The Frozen Expanse

Major Landmarks

The Great Tree — Westmere

Deep within one of Westmere’s largest forests stands an enormous ancient tree surrounded by a broad natural clearing. The forest grows densely again beyond the clearing, making the tree and its open surroundings highly distinctive. The Great Tree is one of Westmere’s most recognizable natural landmarks and can be reached by a Secondary Trail branching from the region’s main travel network. The tree is not inherently magical or supernatural. Its significance comes from its immense age and size, its distinctive appearance, and the ecosystem that has developed around it. The clearing provides a natural gathering place for both people and Entheon.

The Great Windmill — Greenvale

The Great Windmill is an enormous functioning structure located in the agricultural heart of Greenvale. Rather than being a conventional factory with a windmill attached, the entire facility was designed around the concept of a gigantic wind-powered processing plant. Its machinery processes agricultural products produced throughout the surrounding region. Its enormous scale makes it visible from considerable distances across Greenvale’s relatively open farmland. The structure is both an important piece of regional infrastructure and one of Greenvale’s defining landmarks. A Secondary Trail connects the main transportation network to the facility.

Great Hawthorne Lake — Hawthorne

Great Hawthorne Lake is a vast inland freshwater lake that dominates the geography of Hawthorne. Its shoreline contains a mixture of wetlands, forests, beaches, rocky sections, and smaller waterways. Rivers and streams flow into and away from the lake, creating a diverse range of freshwater habitats. Lakecrest City was established directly alongside the lake, making the waterway an important part of the city’s history, commerce, and everyday life. The lake is large enough to significantly influence regional travel. Trails naturally follow portions of its shoreline or route around its edges rather than treating it as an obstacle that can simply be crossed.

The Cascading River — Northvale

A distinctive rocky river system runs through Northvale, descending through a sequence of natural levels. Rather than forming one enormous waterfall, the river repeatedly drops over rocky shelves, creating a series of smaller waterfalls, pools, rapids, and cascades. The resulting landscape resembles a natural staircase carved into the terrain. The Cascading River is both a notable landmark and an important freshwater habitat for Entheon. Trails in the surrounding area follow the river where practical, while some sections require travellers to move around the steeper drops.

The Highreach Hot Springs — Highreach / Dunridge / Northvale

Near the mountainous meeting point of Highreach, Dunridge, and Northvale lies a geothermal area containing a series of naturally heated springs. The springs remain warm despite the surrounding high-altitude environment, where snow and cold weather are common. Steam rising from the pools can be visible against the surrounding mountains, particularly during colder weather. The area is known throughout Kaleo as a place to rest and recover during mountain travel. Different pools vary in temperature and surrounding terrain, and the area also provides a distinctive habitat for Entheon adapted to the combination of geothermal warmth and mountainous conditions. The hot springs are accessible through a Secondary Trail branching from the regional mountain routes.

Lume Port — Lume

Lume’s major city is built around a naturally sheltered coastal harbor and serves as the island’s primary connection to the rest of Kaleo. The city functions as a major trading and transportation hub, with ships and ferries carrying passengers, goods, and supplies between Lume and established coastal destinations throughout Kaleo. The port is not a separate settlement from the city. Lume Port is the central maritime district and defining function of the island’s major city. Outside the city, much of Lume remains relatively undeveloped, allowing substantial areas of the island to remain natural Entheon habitat.

The Frozen Expanse — Isen

Rather than a single constructed landmark, Isen’s defining feature is the immense frozen landscape itself. Vast stretches of tundra, snowfield, permafrost, frozen waterways, and exposed rocky ground extend across the region. During periods of particularly severe weather, visibility can become extremely limited and familiar terrain can become difficult to navigate. The Frozen Expanse is one of the least developed environments in Kaleo. Outside Winterhold and its established travel connections, human infrastructure becomes increasingly scarce. The wilderness itself is what defines Isen.

Trails & Transportation

Main Trails

Main Trails are Kaleo’s principal overland travel routes. A Main Trail generally leads toward or through a major city and passes established settlements along the way. Main Trails are not arranged around Gym progression and do not require travellers to visit the Gyms in geographical order. Trails naturally curve around terrain, waterways, mountains, and other geographical features rather than following perfectly straight lines.

Secondary Trails

Secondary Trails branch away from Main Trails when a destination is not directly along the primary route. They may lead to:

  • smaller settlements
  • landmarks
  • alternative destinations
  • areas of particular natural or Entheon interest

A Secondary Trail does not necessarily lead toward another major city or Gym.

Ferry Routes

Established ferry routes provide maritime transportation, particularly around Lume. Ferries connect existing coastal settlements and cities with Lume, allowing travellers and goods to cross the Central Sea without requiring long overland journeys around it. Ferry routes are separate from the Trail network and are represented geographically as maritime routes rather than roads.

Geographic Principle

Kaleo should feel like a coherent inhabited continent rather than a linear adventure path. Cities, settlements, landmarks, wilderness, Trails, and ferry connections exist because of the geography and needs of the people living there. Gym progression is layered onto this world rather than determining its structure.

Wild Entheon Ecology

Wild Entheon populations vary significantly between regions. Not every Entheon species occurs throughout Kaleo; many have specific natural ranges determined by habitat, climate and environmental conditions. Regional encounter frequency is described using three categories:

  • Common — regularly encountered within suitable habitat.
  • Uncommon — naturally present but encountered less frequently.
  • Rare — unusual encounters even within suitable habitat.

An Entheon being classified as Common, Uncommon or Rare describes its wild encounter frequency, not necessarily its overall population, commercial availability or rarity as a species.

Evolutionary Stages

Wild Entheon can exceptionally be encountered in evolved forms, but these are considerably less common than their earlier stages. Stage 3 Entheon are never encountered as normal wild Entheon. Stage 3 forms represent a level of development reached through training and progression rather than a form encountered naturally in the wild. Entheon whose evolution requires a specific evolutionary item also do not naturally appear in their evolved forms. The required item makes deliberate intervention necessary for that evolution to occur.

Veyli

Veyli is an unusual case. Although classified as a Common Entheon species, naturally occurring Veyli populations are restricted to only a small number of regions. Veyli naturally occur in Westmere and Eastmere, where they are nevertheless Rare encounters. Veyli are adaptable but naturally maintain small populations, meaning that encountering one in the wild is considered fortunate. Veyli encountered elsewhere are likely to be owned, bred, traded or transported individuals rather than members of a natural wild population. Veyli’s evolved forms are never wild encounters, as their evolution requires a specific evolutionary item.

Alphan Entheon

An Alphan Entheon is an unusually large and exceptionally developed individual of its species. Alphan Entheon are not a separate species or evolutionary form. They retain the same basic appearance and characteristics as ordinary members of their species but are noticeably larger and generally more physically imposing. Alpha individuals are usually found toward the upper end of the natural level range for their area and are considerably less common than ordinary encounters. An Alphan Entheon is not inherently hostile, although its size, territorial behavior or confidence may make encounters more dangerous. Alpha status does not override normal evolutionary rules:

  • A Stage 3 Entheon cannot appear as a wild Alpha.
  • An item-dependent evolution cannot appear as a wild Alpha.
  • Alpha status does not create a separate evolutionary line.
  • An Alpha remains an individual of its normal species.

The presence of an Alpha can sometimes be inferred from the behavior of other Entheon in the area, particularly when ordinary individuals avoid or give unusual space to a particular territory.

Wild Entheon Distribution

Wild Entheon are distributed according to the ecology, terrain, climate and level range of each region. Encounter frequency is divided into four categories:

  • Common: Regularly encountered within suitable habitats.
  • Uncommon: Present in the region but encountered less frequently.
  • Rare: Unusual encounters that may require exploration or suitable conditions.
  • Rare Evolution: Evolved Entheon that can naturally occur in the wild despite their rarity.

Some species have additional encounter conditions, such as time of day or geographic exclusivity. Stage 3 Entheon do not occur as wild encounters. Certain special-evolution forms also do not occur naturally in the wild, and Veyli’s evolved forms require specific catalysts and are therefore excluded from wild encounters.

Gym Leaders

Gym Leaders are prominent Trainers who serve as the public representatives of their respective Gyms. Each specializes in a particular Affinity, but their personal identity, profession, battle philosophy and approach to training are not necessarily defined by that Affinity alone. A Gym Leader’s established roster consists of six Entheon. During Gym challenges, Leaders may use an appropriate subset of that roster depending on the challenger’s progression.

Everhope Gym — Rowan Vale

Region: Westmere Affinity: Gale Age: 32

Appearance

Rowan has a lean, athletic build and an easygoing appearance that makes him look more like an experienced outdoorsman than a formal Gym Leader. He has medium-length dark-blond hair that falls loosely around his face and neck, deliberately kept long enough for him to feel the breeze moving through it. He has light grey-blue eyes and a perpetual hint of stubble. His clothing combines practical outdoor gear with a slightly stylish edge: a lightweight weatherproof jacket, fitted shirt, durable trousers and well-worn hiking boots. His jacket is usually left open, even when the weather would suggest otherwise. Rowan likes the feeling of wind in his hair and rarely bothers tying it back unless he genuinely needs to. If the wind is blowing, he is perfectly happy to let it do its thing.

Personality

Rowan is relaxed, observant and quietly confident. He rarely raises his voice and does not particularly care about maintaining the intimidating image some Gym Leaders cultivate. He enjoys talking to challengers and will often have a casual conversation with them before the battle. He is, however, extremely observant. Rowan notices small things: how a Trainer interacts with their Entheon, whether they panic when something goes wrong, and whether they adapt their plans or stubbornly continue with them. He believes that a good Trainer should not need everything to go according to plan.

Background

Rowan grew up around Westmere’s forests and developed a fascination with climbing, hiking and weather patterns. Before becoming a Gym Leader, he worked as an outdoor guide, taking people through some of Westmere’s more difficult wilderness routes. His experience with Gale Entheon began through those expeditions. Rather than seeking out powerful Entheon specifically, he developed relationships with species that naturally accompanied him through the region. Eventually, his reputation as an exceptionally adaptable Trainer led to him being offered the position at Everhope Gym.

Gym Philosophy

“You can’t control the wind. You can only learn how to move with it.”

Rowan sees battling as an exercise in adaptation. A Trainer can enter a battle with the perfect strategy, but the opponent gets to make decisions too. Their Entheon may behave unexpectedly, the environment may change, or a carefully planned attack may simply fail. The important question is not “What was your plan?” It is: “What do you do when your plan stops working?”

Battle Style

Rowan’s battles are fast, mobile and reactive. He prefers Entheon that can reposition themselves, exploit openings and force opponents to continually reconsider their approach. He does not necessarily overwhelm opponents with raw power. Instead, he creates situations where an opponent’s positioning, timing or assumptions become liabilities. His battles tend to feel dynamic rather than predictable.

Gym Assessment

The Everhope Gym does not simply test whether a challenger can defeat Rowan. The Gym’s assessment is built around adaptability and communication. Before the battle, challengers must navigate a wind-based obstacle course with their Entheon. The exact route changes depending on environmental conditions, and some obstacles cannot be overcome simply through strength. Rowan watches how the Trainer communicates with their Entheon:

  • Do they adapt when the original route fails?
  • Do they understand what their Entheon is comfortable doing?
  • Do they give useful commands rather than simply shouting instructions?
  • Do they recognize when their Entheon needs to change approach?
  • Can they remain composed when something unexpected happens?

The final battle then tests the same principle under pressure. A challenger who defeats Rowan through brute force but demonstrates poor communication may receive considerably more criticism than someone who loses narrowly but demonstrates exceptional adaptability.

Established Entheon Roster

Rowan’s established six-Entheon roster consists of six distinct evolutionary lines, with no duplicate evolution families.

  1. Nymbril — Wild/Gale Rowan’s signature partner and the clearest representation of his philosophy. Nymbril’s mobility and ability to exploit its surroundings make it particularly suited to Rowan’s approach to battle.
  2. Rookane — Metal/Gale Rowan’s dedicated aerial specialist. Its combination of aerial control and Metal affinity gives him a different tactical approach from his other mobile Entheon.
  3. Avenn — Gale A highly maneuverable specialist suited to precision attacks, rapid directional changes and controlling the pace of an encounter.
  4. Sovel — Gale A playful and unpredictable member of the team that excels at sudden changes of direction and momentum.
  5. Caelune — Gale/Tide A highly unusual aerial Entheon whose biological jetpack allows it to launch itself rapidly through the battlefield and make sharp turns. Its secondary Tide Affinity gives Rowan additional tactical options.
  6. Quiblet — Flame Rowan’s wildcard. Although it does not possess the Gale Affinity, Quiblet is airborne and fits his philosophy of movement and adaptability. Its inclusion also prevents his team from becoming overly predictable.

Entheon Levels

Rowan’s established Entheon range from approximately Lv. 10–16. The exact levels and composition used during a Gym challenge depend on the challenger’s progression, allowing Rowan to provide an appropriate assessment without necessarily revealing or using the full strength of his established team. His full six-Entheon roster represents Rowan the Trainer; the smaller Gym battle roster represents Rowan as an assessor.

Gullhaven Gym — Maya Corwin

Region: Seawick Affinity: Tide Age: 29

Appearance

Maya has the athletic physique you’d expect from someone who has spent years surfing, swimming and working as a lifeguard. She has sun-kissed skin, bright blue-green eyes and long dark-auburn hair, usually worn in a loose braid or ponytail while she is working. Away from work, she is just as likely to leave it loose. Her clothing is practical rather than ceremonial. She typically wears a sleeveless rash guard, lightweight board shorts, water-resistant sandals and a weathered rescue jacket that can be thrown on when needed. A whistle hangs around her neck almost constantly, largely because she has worn one for so long that she barely notices it anymore. Her battered surfboard is rarely far away, covered in stickers and the accumulated marks of years spent in the water.

Personality

Maya is energetic, outgoing and naturally approachable. She is competitive in a cheerful way and might challenge someone she has just met to a race along the beach without giving it a second thought. That easygoing attitude changes immediately when someone’s safety is involved. Her experience as a lifeguard has given her a strong protective instinct, and she takes reckless behaviour seriously. Maya doesn’t equate courage with ignoring danger. She respects people who understand their own limitations and recognize when circumstances have changed. Losing a battle doesn’t particularly bother her; failing to learn from one does.

Background

Maya was raised in Gullhaven and grew up around the sea. Swimming became second nature to her at an early age, and she discovered surfing during her early teens. She eventually trained as a lifeguard and spent years working along Gullhaven’s beaches, becoming one of the area’s most experienced rescue swimmers. That career also brought her into frequent contact with Tide Entheon. Many of her strongest relationships with Entheon developed naturally through beach patrols, rescues and countless hours spent in and around the water rather than through deliberately seeking out powerful battle partners. Her reputation as both an accomplished Trainer and an experienced lifeguard eventually resulted in her appointment as Gullhaven’s Gym Leader.

Gym Philosophy

“The water doesn’t care how strong you are. Learn to read it.”

For Maya, Tide represents flow, adaptation and awareness. She doesn’t believe the strongest Trainer is necessarily the one capable of producing the most powerful attack. A capable Trainer understands what is happening around them, recognizes when circumstances shift and knows when to advance, change direction or give ground. Maya often compares battling to surfing. A surfer doesn’t defeat a wave through brute force. They read its movement, work with it and recognize the precise moment when they can turn that movement to their advantage.

Battle Style

Maya’s battles revolve around momentum and constant adjustment. Rather than committing herself to either offense or defense, she moves fluidly between the two. Her Entheon may retreat deliberately, reposition themselves and then suddenly turn that apparent loss of ground into an offensive opportunity. She likes creating situations in which the opponent has to make difficult choices: whether to attack immediately or wait, whether to pursue or reposition, and whether to persist with an existing strategy or abandon it. As a result, her battles tend to develop a distinctive rhythm, alternating between periods of pressure and sudden changes in tempo.

Gym Assessment

The Gullhaven Gym incorporates the surrounding water into its assessment. Challengers are required to work alongside their Entheon through a series of aquatic exercises, including navigating changing currents, reaching designated locations and responding to simulated rescue situations. Physical strength alone isn’t what Maya is evaluating. She pays particular attention to whether the Trainer understands their Entheon’s abilities and limitations, responds effectively when conditions change, recognizes when an approach has become unsafe and communicates without trying to micromanage every action. Most importantly, she looks at whether a Trainer will prioritize their Entheon’s wellbeing over simply completing the challenge. A challenger who recklessly forces an Entheon through a dangerous situation can fail the assessment even if they technically complete the obstacle. To Maya, recognizing when to stop is evidence of good judgment rather than weakness.

Established Entheon Roster

Maya’s established team contains six distinct evolutionary lines, with no repeated evolution families.

  1. Vessa — Tide Maya’s original partner and the Entheon that accompanied her during the early years when she was learning to surf.
  2. Koroa — Tide A powerful swimmer capable of manipulating the battlefield through water and controlling the space around an opponent.
  3. Selka — Tide A quick and agile aquatic Entheon that gives Maya an option focused on rapid repositioning.
  4. Veysha — Tide A less conventional member of Maya’s team, providing a tactical option that opponents may not immediately anticipate.
  5. Mirel — Tide A versatile member capable of helping Maya transition between defensive and offensive approaches while controlling the battle’s tempo.
  6. Pirello — Tide (Ace) Maya’s strongest and most trusted battle partner. Pirello embodies her approach to battling: understand the current situation, wait for the right opening, and then use the momentum of the battle to make a decisive push.

Entheon Levels

Maya’s established Entheon range from approximately Lv. 14–20. The levels and specific members she uses in an official Gym challenge can vary according to the challenger’s progression. This allows her to provide an appropriate assessment without necessarily revealing or deploying the full strength of her established team. Her complete roster represents Maya as an experienced Trainer, while the smaller team used during a Gym challenge represents Maya in her role as an assessor.

Thermalis Gym — Darius Voss

Region: Highreach Affinity: Fire/Stone Age: 41

Appearance

Darius is broad-shouldered and powerfully built, with the physique of someone who has spent decades doing physical work rather than training for appearance. He has dark, slightly greying hair, a short beard and deep brown eyes. He usually wears a heavy work apron over a simple sleeveless shirt, reinforced work trousers and sturdy boots. His forearms show the occasional small scar or burn from years around furnaces and stonework. Protective gloves are usually tucked into his belt rather than worn unless he is actually working. His clothing is practical and well-used, but he takes pride in keeping his tools in excellent condition.

Personality

Darius is calm, patient and straightforward. He isn’t particularly interested in impressing people and has little tolerance for unnecessary theatrics. He can initially come across as intimidating because of his size and reserved manner, but he is actually remarkably approachable. He enjoys teaching people how things work and has a habit of giving practical advice even when nobody asked for it. He believes quality comes from repetition, patience and accepting that mistakes are part of learning. He is also stubborn. Once Darius believes something is worth doing, convincing him otherwise can be extremely difficult.

Background

Darius grew up in Thermalis and comes from a family of craftspeople who have worked with the region’s volcanic stone and mineral resources for generations. He initially became a blacksmith, eventually expanding into stonework and specialized equipment used by Trainers and Entheon researchers. His work brought him into close contact with Fire and Stone Entheon, and he gradually developed a reputation as an exceptionally capable Trainer. Rather than abandoning his trade when he became Gym Leader, Darius incorporated the Gym into his workshop. The Thermalis Gym therefore doubles as a functioning forge and stoneworking facility.

Gym Philosophy

“Heat reveals what something is made of. Pressure reveals what it can become.”

Darius believes that strength isn’t something you simply possess. It is something you build. Fire represents transformation: applying enough energy to change something. Stone represents endurance: remaining stable when that energy and pressure are applied. His philosophy extends to Trainers as much as Entheon. He doesn’t expect challengers to be perfect. He wants to see whether they can withstand setbacks, learn from mistakes and continue improving rather than falling apart when their first plan fails.

Battle Style

Darius favours controlled pressure and endurance. His battles tend to begin deliberately rather than explosively. He establishes his position, forces the opponent to commit and gradually increases the pressure. His Entheon combine defensive resilience with powerful bursts of Fire or Stone attacks. Rather than constantly switching tactics, Darius is comfortable allowing a battle to become a contest of endurance—provided he has prepared his Entheon properly for it. He is particularly good at punishing opponents who become impatient.

Gym Assessment

The Thermalis Gym assessment takes place throughout Darius’s working forge. Challengers must complete a series of practical tasks alongside their Entheon, involving heat, pressure and stone. These aren’t simply strength tests. Some tasks require careful control, others require persistence, and some require recognizing when continuing to push would be counterproductive. Darius observes:

  • Can the Trainer remain calm when something goes wrong?
  • Do they understand the difference between persistence and recklessness?
  • Can they adjust their approach without abandoning the objective?
  • Do they trust their Entheon to perform tasks independently?
  • Can they work patiently toward a result rather than rushing for immediate success?

The final battle tests the same qualities. Darius wants to see whether the challenger can take pressure without losing their composure.

Established Entheon Roster

Darius’s established team consists of six distinct evolutionary lines, with no repeated evolution families.

  1. Dovik — Stone A dependable Stone Entheon that represents the endurance and stability at the heart of Darius’s philosophy.
  2. Arko — Flame Darius’s straightforward offensive specialist, providing a sharp contrast to his more defensive Entheon.
  3. Braska — Fire/Stone The first member of his roster to combine both of his specialized Affinities, representing the balance between transformation and endurance.
  4. Brindrel — Verdant/Stone A less obvious member of Darius’s team whose secondary Stone Affinity makes it a natural fit despite its Verdant aspect. It gives him an option that opponents may not immediately expect from a Fire/Stone Gym.
  5. Tarnelle — Stone/Metal Darius’s defensive specialist. Its Metal Affinity complements its Stone characteristics and reinforces his emphasis on resilience and sustained pressure.
  6. Quivane — Flame/Volt (Ace) Darius’s most trusted battle partner and his greatest tactical surprise. Its Volt Affinity gives his otherwise methodical team an additional dimension, allowing him to punish challengers who assume they have completely prepared for a Fire/Stone specialist.

Entheon Levels

Darius’s established Entheon range from approximately Lv. 20–26. The levels and specific members he uses in an official Gym challenge can vary according to the challenger’s progression. This allows him to provide an appropriate assessment without necessarily revealing or deploying the full strength of his established team. His complete roster represents Darius as an experienced Trainer, while the smaller team used during a Gym challenge represents Darius in his role as an assessor.

Harveston Gym — Elara Greenfield

Region: Westmere Affinity: Verdant Age: 36

Appearance

Elara has a sturdy, healthy build from years of agricultural work, with warm brown skin, green eyes and thick chestnut hair that she usually wears in a loose braid.
