Her clothing is practical and comfortable: a linen shirt with rolled-up sleeves, durable trousers, sturdy work boots and a weathered gardening apron. Small traces of her work are almost always visible—soil beneath her fingernails, a few leaves caught in her hair, or a smudge of dirt on one sleeve. She doesn’t particularly care. Rather than wearing anything resembling a traditional Gym Leader uniform, Elara dresses much like the farmers and horticulturalists she works alongside.

Personality

Elara is warm, patient and quietly stubborn. She has a nurturing personality and tends to treat people much like she treats plants: give them the right conditions, give them room to grow, and don’t expect them to develop at exactly the same pace. She’s friendly and approachable, but her patience has limits. She has little sympathy for people who mistreat Entheon, plants or the land around them. She is also surprisingly competitive once a battle begins. Her gentle demeanour shouldn’t be mistaken for passivity.

Background

Elara grew up around Harveston’s agricultural communities and became involved in farming from an early age. Her family maintained a mixed farm, growing crops and raising produce suited to the region’s fertile land. As she grew older, she became particularly interested in the relationship between agriculture and Verdant Entheon. She eventually began working with local farmers to understand how Entheon could help maintain crops, manage difficult terrain and protect farmland from wild creatures. Her success eventually led to her becoming Harveston’s Gym Leader. She still maintains her own agricultural plots, and the Gym incorporates working gardens and cultivated land rather than being separated from the community around it.

Gym Philosophy

“You can’t force something to grow. You can only give it what it needs.”

Elara believes that good Trainers understand growth, patience and individual potential. Every Entheon develops differently. Some thrive under pressure. Others need time and encouragement. Some are naturally aggressive, while others are better suited to support or defensive roles. A Trainer who tries to force every Entheon into the same mould will eventually limit their own team.

Battle Style

Elara prefers sustainable pressure and battlefield control. Rather than immediately trying to overwhelm an opponent, she establishes favourable conditions and gradually makes the battlefield work against them. Her Entheon use terrain, positioning and sustained effects to restrict an opponent’s options while maintaining their own resilience. She is particularly fond of battles where her opponent becomes increasingly frustrated because their original strategy gradually stops working.

Gym Assessment

The Harveston Gym assessment takes place among cultivated gardens, fields and greenhouses. Challengers must work with their Entheon to complete a series of tasks involving cultivation, navigation and maintaining living environments. Some challenges require physical strength, while others reward patience, observation and careful cooperation. Elara watches for:

  • Does the Trainer understand what their Entheon actually needs?
  • Can they adapt their approach to different individuals?
  • Are they willing to let their Entheon contribute rather than controlling every action?
  • Can they recognize gradual progress rather than expecting immediate results?
  • Do they demonstrate care for the environment around them?

The final battle tests whether the challenger can maintain a coherent strategy over time rather than simply attempting to end the battle as quickly as possible.

Established Entheon Roster

Elara’s established team consists of six distinct evolutionary lines, with no repeated evolution families.

  1. Meliu — Verdant A gentle and plant-associated Entheon that reflects Elara’s nurturing approach and her connection to horticulture.
  2. Orven — Verdant A distinct single-stage Verdant Entheon that gives Elara another established species without relying on an evolutionary line already represented on her team.
  3. Mirel — Tide/Verdant A versatile member that brings a secondary Affinity into Elara’s team while maintaining a strong connection to cultivated and natural environments.
  4. Rilsa — Verdant/Tide A natural fit for Harveston’s fertile environment and a member that gives Elara additional options through its Tide Affinity.
  5. Brindrel — Verdant/Stone The more physically imposing member of Elara’s team. Its Stone Affinity provides additional resilience and allows her to shift away from purely defensive Verdant strategies.
  6. Virenne — Verdant/Gale (Ace) Elara’s fastest and most mobile team member. Virenne gives her battle style an important change of pace and prevents opponents from assuming that her team will simply rely on slow, sustained pressure.

Entheon Levels

Elara’s established Entheon range from approximately Lv. 24–30. The levels and specific members she uses in an official Gym challenge can vary according to the challenger’s progression. This allows her to provide an appropriate assessment without necessarily revealing or deploying the full strength of her established team. Her complete roster represents Elara as an experienced Trainer, while the smaller team used during a Gym challenge represents Elara in her role as an assessor.

Lakecrest Gym — Lena Hartwell

Region: Westmere Affinity: Volt Age: 31

Appearance

Lena has a lean, athletic build and an energetic presence. She has dark, almost black hair worn around shoulder length, with several vivid red-dyed streaks running through the lower layers. The red is deliberately a little uneven rather than perfectly salon-styled, giving her a casual rocker’s aesthetic. Her eyes are bright hazel, and she usually wears a mischievous expression that makes it difficult to tell whether she is about to explain something technical or make a joke. Her clothing blends practical electrical workwear with a distinctly modern rock-inspired style: a fitted black utility jacket, dark work trousers, sturdy boots and a tool belt containing various small instruments. She sometimes wears a band-style shirt underneath her jacket, and insulated gloves are usually shoved into one of her pockets rather than actually worn. Small electrical motifs appear on some of her equipment, but she avoids dressing like a literal walking lightning bolt.

Personality

Lena is energetic, curious and extremely quick-thinking. She loves figuring out how things work and has a tendency to start explaining an interesting technical problem before realizing the person she is talking to may have absolutely no idea what she is talking about. She is playful and enjoys teasing challengers, particularly when they become too predictable. Lena believes almost every problem can be solved by understanding the system. However, she also knows that understanding a pattern is not enough. Once an opponent has figured out what she is doing, she expects herself and her Entheon to be capable of changing the system before it can be exploited. Her enthusiasm occasionally leads her to overcomplicate things that could have been solved much more simply.

Background

Lena grew up around Lakecrest and became fascinated with electrical systems at a young age. She studied electrical engineering and eventually returned to the region, where she began working on power infrastructure and small-scale technology projects. Her work brought her into contact with Volt Entheon, particularly those living around the lake’s infrastructure and nearby settlements. She eventually began designing equipment that allowed communities to make better use of naturally occurring electrical energy while also developing as a Trainer. Outside of her engineering work, Lena has a strong interest in rock music and occasionally performs with a local band. When the Lakecrest Gym needed a new Leader, Lena was an obvious candidate. The Gym itself doubles as a demonstration workshop, with generators, electrical systems and experimental equipment incorporated into the challenge.

Gym Philosophy

“Understand the system. Then break the pattern.”

Lena believes that battles are problems to be solved. She encourages Trainers to observe what is happening, identify patterns and recognize what their opponent is trying to accomplish. But she also believes that understanding a pattern isn’t enough. Once an opponent has figured out what you are doing, you need to be capable of changing the system before they exploit it.

Battle Style

Lena’s battles focus on speed, disruption and exploiting predictable behaviour. She likes forcing opponents to react rather than allowing them to establish their preferred rhythm. Her Entheon may rapidly change targets, reposition or use Volt techniques to create openings for another member. She particularly enjoys punishing Trainers who rely too heavily on a single strategy. Her battles can feel chaotic to inexperienced opponents, but there is usually a very deliberate structure underneath the apparent chaos.

Gym Assessment

The Lakecrest Gym is effectively a giant electrical puzzle. Challengers must navigate a series of interconnected rooms where switches, circuits and mechanical systems affect one another. Activating one system may disable another, open a route elsewhere or change the environment in a different section. The challenge isn’t designed to test technical knowledge. Everything necessary can be learned through observation. Lena watches for:

  • Does the Trainer observe before acting?
  • Can they recognize patterns?
  • Do they learn from previous mistakes?
  • Can they adapt when their assumptions are wrong?
  • Do they communicate effectively with their Entheon?
  • Can they distinguish between a complicated solution and a genuinely useful one?

The final battle tests the same principle. Lena deliberately establishes patterns during the battle and then breaks them, forcing the challenger to recognize that their opponent has adapted.

Established Entheon Roster

Lena’s established team consists of six distinct evolutionary lines, with no repeated evolution families.

  1. Tivvi — Volt A small, hyperactive electric Entheon that perfectly matches Lena’s energetic personality. Tivvi is constantly attracted to electrical equipment and can often be found investigating the Gym’s machinery.
  2. Keln — Metal A practical fit for Lena’s engineering background. Keln gives her a strong connection to machinery and the Metal Affinity while providing a different combat identity from her Volt Entheon.
  3. Avenn — Gale Lena’s fast aerial specialist. Its exceptional maneuverability gives her a way to control positioning and introduces another dimension to her battle style.
  4. Rookane — Metal/Gale A more substantial aerial threat that combines mobility with Metal resilience. Rookane allows Lena to maintain pressure from above while continuing to challenge an opponent’s assumptions about her team.
  5. Veyro — Gale/Volt A highly mobile Volt-affiliated aerial Entheon and a natural expression of Lena’s personality. Veyro’s affinity for strong winds and thunderstorms makes it particularly well suited to Lena’s energetic, unconventional style.
  6. Stage 2 Dragon-line Entheon — Dragon (Ace) Lena’s hidden ace and the member she reserves for exceptional challengers.

Ace Selection

Lena does not normally use her Dragon-line Entheon during standard Gym challenges. She reserves it for exceptional Trainers—particularly challengers who arrive with a reputation for being unusually strong, or those whose confidence crosses into outright arrogance. Sometimes Lena will have heard stories about a challenger beforehand. Other times, she recognizes it during the assessment: a challenger who dismisses her earlier Entheon, assumes they have figured out her strategy, or begins treating victory as inevitable. That is when Lena brings out her ace. The Dragon-line Entheon represents the ultimate expression of her Gym philosophy. Lena deliberately allows opponents to identify patterns in her battle style, only to completely break those patterns with an Entheon they were not expecting. People who have witnessed Lena use her Dragon-line ace often talk about the experience afterward, contributing to its growing reputation among Trainers.

Entheon Levels

Lena’s established Entheon range from approximately Lv. 28–34. The levels and specific members she uses in an official Gym challenge can vary according to the challenger’s progression. Her Dragon-line Stage 2 Entheon is generally reserved for exceptional challengers rather than ordinary Gym battles. Her complete roster represents Lena as an experienced Trainer, while the smaller team used during a Gym challenge represents Lena in her role as an assessor.

Northreach Gym — Magnus Veyr

Region: Highreach Affinity: Metal/Frost Age: 44

Appearance

Magnus is tall, broad-shouldered and heavily built, with a weathered appearance that reflects years spent working in the northern climate. He has pale skin, steel-grey eyes and thick dark-blond hair beginning to grey at the temples. His beard is kept short and neatly trimmed. He dresses for practicality rather than style: a heavy insulated coat, reinforced trousers, sturdy winter boots and thick gloves. Beneath the outer layers he usually wears a simple dark shirt and a reinforced utility harness. His clothing contains subtle Metal detailing, including reinforced buckles, clasps and protective plates. He carries himself with an almost military-like posture, though he has never actually served in the military.

Personality

Magnus is quiet, disciplined and exceptionally patient. He rarely raises his voice and dislikes unnecessary theatrics. He listens carefully before speaking and has a habit of considering a problem from several angles before committing to a decision. Despite his imposing appearance, he is not particularly intimidating once someone gets to know him. He has a dry sense of humour that tends to appear unexpectedly. Magnus respects preparation above almost everything else. He doesn’t believe that courage means charging into danger. To him, courage is knowing what you’re facing and being prepared to endure it anyway.

Background

Magnus grew up in Northreach and spent much of his early life working in the region’s mining and metalworking industries. The harsh environment taught him the importance of preparation, equipment and endurance. A poorly maintained tool could become a serious problem when working far from civilization, and a failure to prepare for sudden changes in weather could be dangerous. His relationship with Metal and Frost Entheon developed naturally through this lifestyle. He eventually became a specialist in cold-weather equipment and reinforced tools used by explorers, miners and Trainers travelling through Highreach. His reputation as a Trainer eventually led to him becoming Northreach’s Gym Leader.

Gym Philosophy

“Steel is forged by what it survives. Frost hardens those it faces.”

Magnus believes that strength is not demonstrated by avoiding adversity, but by enduring it and coming out stronger. Metal represents discipline, structure and the ability to withstand pressure. Frost represents hardship: something that cannot simply be ignored or overcome through brute force. His Gym therefore tests whether a Trainer can keep functioning when things become difficult. He doesn’t particularly care whether a challenger has the perfect strategy from the beginning. He cares whether they can adapt, endure setbacks and keep making good decisions under pressure.

Battle Style

Magnus favours attrition, defensive positioning and controlled counterattacks. His battles rarely begin explosively. He establishes a defensive position, watches how the opponent attacks and gradually forces them to expend resources. His Metal Entheon provide durability and defensive pressure, while Frost Entheon create opportunities to slow, restrict or disrupt opponents. Magnus is particularly fond of turning an opponent’s aggression against them. If a challenger repeatedly throws themselves at his defenses without adapting, he will happily let them exhaust themselves.

Gym Assessment

Northreach Gym is designed around a simulated northern expedition. Challengers must navigate a cold environment while managing limited resources, changing paths and mechanical obstacles. Some sections require their Entheon to assist directly, while others test the Trainer’s ability to recognize when their current approach is becoming inefficient. Magnus observes:

  • Does the Trainer prepare before acting?
  • Can they remain calm when conditions change?
  • Do they understand their Entheon’s strengths and limitations?
  • Can they conserve resources rather than constantly attacking?
  • Do they know when to retreat and reassess?
  • Can they remain effective when their original strategy fails?

The final battle is deliberately longer and more methodical than the earlier Gym battles. Magnus isn’t looking for the Trainer who can produce the biggest attack. He’s looking for the Trainer who can still think clearly after everything has gone wrong.

Established Entheon Roster

Magnus’s established team consists of six distinct evolutionary lines, with no repeated evolution families.

  1. Keln — Metal A sturdy Metal Entheon and Magnus’s dependable opener. Keln represents the simple reliability that Magnus values in both tools and partners.
  2. Talvi — Frost A surprisingly fast Frost specialist. Its playful appearance contrasts with the more serious members of Magnus’s team, making it an effective way to disrupt an opponent’s expectations.
  3. Pellune — Frost/Mystic A more technical Frost fighter capable of combining its cold-based abilities with Mystic techniques. This gives Magnus a tactical option that doesn’t rely purely on physical endurance.
  4. Rookane — Metal/Gale Magnus’s mobile Metal specialist. Its Gale Affinity gives the otherwise methodical team a much-needed increase in mobility and allows it to attack from unexpected angles.
  5. Fennovar — Frost/Stone A heavily built Frost Entheon whose Stone characteristics make it exceptionally durable. Fennovar represents the literal combination of cold and physical resilience that defines Magnus’s Gym.
  6. Ruun — Frost/Umbral (Ace) Magnus’s rarest and most formidable partner. Ruun is a solitary, territorial dark yeti that normally avoids settlements and has an almost folkloric reputation among people living near its habitat. Its Frost abilities combine with Umbral energy to allow it to disappear into snowstorms and darkness.

Entheon Levels

Magnus’s established Entheon range from approximately Lv. 34–40. His earlier team members are used for ordinary Gym assessments, while Ruun is reserved for particularly capable challengers and represents the upper end of Magnus’s established strength. Ruun’s inclusion is not because Magnus simply happened to capture a powerful rare Entheon. The two have a long-standing relationship built on mutual respect, and Magnus is one of the few people Ruun willingly fights alongside. Many Trainers know Magnus has a “snow beast” somewhere in his team, but relatively few have actually seen him use it.

Fairhaven Gym — Celeste Laurent

Region: Eastmere Affinity: Mystic Age: 38

Appearance

Celeste is an elegant woman with a graceful, almost effortlessly poised presence. She has long, dark auburn hair that falls well past her shoulders, usually worn loose or gathered into an elegant half-up style. Her eyes are a striking violet-grey, and she has a refined complexion and an athletic but distinctly feminine build. Her clothing resembles sophisticated evening wear adapted for movement rather than a traditional Trainer’s outfit: a fitted dark dress with layered fabric panels, elegant boots and a tailored jacket that she removes before serious battles. Subtle silver jewellery and violet gemstones complete the look. Nothing about her appearance is excessive; everything is carefully chosen. There is something inherently captivating about Celeste. She knows exactly how to use eye contact, posture and a perfectly timed smile, and she is quite comfortable allowing someone else to wonder whether she is flirting with them or simply enjoying watching them become distracted.

Personality

Celeste is sophisticated, confident and extraordinarily perceptive. She is charming without being overly familiar, flirtatious without being crude and enjoys making people slightly unsure of themselves. She has an excellent sense of humour and genuinely enjoys intelligent conversation. She is particularly fond of people who can surprise her. Celeste rarely reveals everything she is thinking. Even when she is being completely honest, she has a habit of saying things in a way that leaves people wondering whether there was another meaning hidden underneath. She is not cruel or manipulative by nature. Her fascination with psychology comes from genuine curiosity about people. She also has a surprisingly warm side that she shows primarily toward people she trusts and her Entheon.

Background

Celeste grew up in Fairhaven’s upper social circles but deliberately chose not to pursue the conventional career expected of her. She became fascinated with Mystic Entheon and eventually studied perception, cognition and the ways humans and Entheon interpret their surroundings. Over time, she developed into an accomplished Trainer whose battles became known for their psychological complexity. Her reputation eventually brought her to the Fairhaven Gym. Rather than turning the Gym into a conventional battle facility, Celeste transformed it into an elegant series of salons, mirrored chambers and carefully constructed environments where challengers are forced to question what they are seeing.

Gym Philosophy

“The moment you believe you understand what you see, you’ve already given me an advantage.”

Celeste believes that perception is one of the most powerful weapons a Trainer possesses. A battle isn’t simply about knowing what an Entheon can do. It is about understanding what your opponent wants you to believe they are doing. She teaches that confidence is useful, but certainty is dangerous.

Battle Style

Celeste specializes in misdirection, prediction and psychological pressure. She deliberately creates uncertainty about her next move, using her Entheon’s different abilities and secondary Affinities to make opponents question their assumptions. Rather than overwhelming opponents immediately, she encourages them to commit to an interpretation of the battle and then punishes them for it. Her Dragons provide an important contrast: when Celeste decides that subtlety has served its purpose, she can suddenly bring overwhelming physical power onto the field.

Gym Assessment

The Fairhaven Gym is less a physical obstacle course and more a test of perception and judgment. Challengers move through a sequence of elegant rooms containing mirrors, illusions, misleading pathways and situations in which the obvious solution isn’t necessarily the correct one. Celeste observes:

  • Can the Trainer distinguish observation from assumption?
  • Do they notice inconsistencies?
  • Can they remain composed when they aren’t certain what is happening?
  • Do they understand their Entheon’s individual behaviour rather than relying solely on species expectations?
  • Can they recognize when an opponent is deliberately trying to manipulate their decisions?
  • Most importantly, can they change their interpretation when new information appears?

The final battle is designed around the same principle. Celeste wants the challenger to understand that being fooled once is not failure; refusing to reconsider after discovering you were fooled is.

Established Entheon Roster

Celeste’s established team consists of six distinct evolutionary lines, with no repeated evolution families.

  1. Veysha — Mystic/Umbral An unsettling Mystic Entheon whose Umbral Affinity complements Celeste’s emphasis on misdirection and perception. Veysha allows her to introduce uncertainty from the very beginning of a battle.
  2. Thessane — Mystic/Frost An elegant Mystic/Frost Entheon that combines refined Mystic techniques with Frost-based control. Thessane provides Celeste with a more methodical option when subtle disruption isn’t enough.
  3. Elvarin — Mystic/Radiant The shrine-maiden-like member of Celeste’s team. Elvarin’s serene presence and spiritual nature make it an especially natural fit for Celeste’s sophisticated aesthetic and Mystic specialization.
  4. Lumaryn — Radiant/Mystic A powerful Stage 2 Entheon combining Radiant and Mystic Affinities. Lumaryn gives Celeste another avenue for perception and support while providing a markedly different presence from Elvarin despite their shared secondary Affinity.
  5. Yori — Dragon Celeste’s first Dragon and a deliberate escalation in raw physical power. Yori provides a dramatic contrast to the more controlled and deceptive members of her team.
  6. Uveryn — Mystic/Dragon (Ace) Celeste’s ultimate battle partner. Uveryn combines her signature Mystic Affinity with the overwhelming potential of a Dragon, making it the perfect culmination of her battle philosophy.

Entheon Levels

Celeste’s established Entheon range from approximately Lv. 40–46. Her earlier members allow her to conduct a conventional Gym assessment without immediately revealing the full strength of her roster. The later members provide progressively greater pressure, culminating in Uveryn at the upper end of her established range. Celeste’s Dragons are not simply there to provide overwhelming power. She uses them to punish challengers who become too certain that they have correctly understood her strategy. By the time Uveryn enters the field, Celeste expects the challenger to have learned an important lesson from the Gym: Knowing what you see is not the same as understanding what it means.

Part II — World Reference

6. Human–Entheon Relationship

Entheon are sentient individuals with their own instincts, emotions, preferences and personalities. Becoming a Trainer does not turn an Entheon into a possession or eliminate its individual agency. A successful Trainer–Entheon relationship is built on trust, understanding and cooperation, rather than obedience alone.

6.1 Individual Agency

Every Entheon is an individual. Species characteristics influence behaviour, instincts and capabilities, but they do not completely determine an individual’s personality. Two Entheon of the same species can have dramatically different temperaments, preferences and relationships with humans. An Entheon’s Nature and Personality influence how it behaves and how it responds to its Trainer. Some Entheon are naturally eager to battle. Others may be cautious, playful, stubborn, independent or easily distracted. A Trainer is expected to understand these differences rather than treating every Entheon identically. Entheon are capable of:

  • forming preferences
  • experiencing emotions
  • developing friendships
  • becoming attached to people and other Entheon
  • disliking certain situations or individuals
  • learning from experience
  • making decisions independently
  • expressing disagreement

An Entheon remains an individual regardless of whether it is wild, partnered with a Trainer or participating in a battle.

6.2 Trainer–Entheon Bonds

The relationship between a Trainer and their Entheon develops over time. Bond represents the strength and quality of that relationship. Bond can grow through:

  • spending time together
  • travelling together
  • battling together
  • caring for an Entheon’s needs
  • respecting its personality
  • successfully overcoming difficult situations together
  • understanding how an individual Entheon prefers to train and fight
  • treating the Entheon as a partner rather than a tool

Bond can also deteriorate. Repeatedly ignoring an Entheon’s needs, forcing it into situations it strongly objects to, treating it poorly or consistently disregarding its individuality can damage the relationship. High Bond does not mean unquestioning obedience. A highly bonded Entheon may actually be more comfortable disagreeing with its Trainer because the relationship is strong enough to withstand disagreement.

6.3 Commands and Cooperation

During battle, a Trainer normally gives commands while their Entheon decides how best to execute them. A command is not equivalent to direct control. An experienced Trainer does not necessarily need to describe every movement. Over time, Trainer and Entheon can develop an understanding that allows the Entheon to interpret a command according to the situation. For example, a Trainer might simply say:

“Get behind it.”

An experienced Entheon may understand the intended objective and determine the best way to accomplish it. An Entheon can also refuse or disregard a command. This is uncommon when the Trainer and Entheon have a strong relationship, but it can happen when:

  • the command conflicts with the Entheon’s instincts
  • the Entheon believes the command would cause unnecessary harm
  • the Trainer has repeatedly ignored the Entheon’s objections
  • the Entheon is frightened or emotionally overwhelmed
  • the command fundamentally conflicts with the Entheon’s personality
  • the Entheon has lost trust in its Trainer

A Trainer cannot simply assume that owning a Capture Crystal guarantees obedience.

6.4 Capture and Consent

Capture establishes a formal Trainer–Entheon partnership, but the Capture Crystal does not erase an Entheon’s individuality. An Entheon that willingly joins a Trainer is fundamentally different from one that is repeatedly forced into situations it does not accept. The Crystal provides the mechanism through which a Trainer can safely carry and transport an Entheon, but it does not dictate the Entheon’s personality, emotions or loyalty. A Trainer’s responsibility therefore continues after capture. Capturing an Entheon is the beginning of a relationship, not the completion of one.

6.5 Life Outside Battle

Entheon are part of their Trainer’s everyday lives. They may travel together, eat together, train together and spend time together without battling. Depending on the individual Entheon and the Trainer’s circumstances, an Entheon may spend much of its time outside its Capture Crystal. Entheon are also capable of forming relationships with other Entheon belonging to the same Trainer. A team is therefore not simply a collection of six combatants. It can become a genuine social group with friendships, rivalries and individual dynamics. A Trainer who understands these relationships can often build a much more cohesive team.

6.6 Strong Relationships

Exceptional Trainer–Entheon relationships are characterized by mutual understanding rather than absolute obedience. A highly experienced Trainer may know an Entheon’s habits well enough to anticipate what it will do without giving explicit instructions. Likewise, an Entheon with a strong Bond may understand what its Trainer is attempting even when a command is incomplete. This can create remarkable coordination during battle, but it is not a supernatural connection. The advantage comes from experience, trust and familiarity. A Trainer with six extremely obedient Entheon is not automatically stronger than a Trainer whose six partners have greater individual personalities. Likewise, a high Bond does not guarantee victory. It simply means that the Trainer and Entheon have developed a deeper understanding of one another.

6.7 What Makes a Good Trainer?

Being a successful Trainer is therefore not primarily about collecting powerful Entheon. A good Trainer understands that each Entheon is different. They learn:

  • what motivates each partner
  • what makes them uncomfortable
  • how they prefer to train
  • how they behave under pressure
  • what they are naturally good at
  • where they need support
  • when to push them
  • when to give them space

The strongest Trainer–Entheon partnerships are those where both sides continue to grow. A Trainer doesn’t simply make an Entheon stronger. The Entheon can also change the Trainer. The strongest Trainer–Entheon partnerships are therefore not those where power is irrelevant. They are those where power and partnership reinforce one another. 

7. Affinity in the World

Affinity is one of the fundamental characteristics of Entheon. It influences an Entheon’s biology, natural abilities and combat identity, but it does not determine its personality, intelligence, temperament or morality. An Affinity should be understood as a natural characteristic of an Entheon rather than a simple classification of its behaviour.

7.1 What Affinity Represents

Every Entheon possesses one or two Affinities. An Affinity represents the type of energy, phenomenon or natural force with which an Entheon is inherently associated. Affinity can influence:

  • an Entheon’s physical and biological characteristics
  • naturally occurring abilities
  • the techniques it can learn or use
  • its interactions with other Affinities
  • the environments in which it naturally thrives
  • its overall combat identity

An Entheon’s Affinity does not completely determine its appearance or behaviour. Two Entheon with the same Affinity can look, behave and fight very differently. Likewise, an Entheon’s Affinity does not determine its personality or morality. A creature with an Umbral Affinity is not inherently sinister, just as a creature with a Radiant Affinity is not inherently benevolent.

7.2 The Thirteen Affinities

The world recognizes thirteen established Affinities:

  • Flame
  • Tide
  • Verdant
  • Gale
  • Volt
  • Frost
  • Stone
  • Metal
  • Mystic
  • Radiant
  • Umbral
  • Wild
  • Dragon

Affinities can exist individually or in combination. Dual-Affinity Entheon possess characteristics associated with both Affinities. The combination does not necessarily mean that both Affinities are equally expressed in every aspect of the Entheon’s biology or abilities.

7.3 Affinity and Humans

Affinity is primarily an Entheon characteristic. Humans do not naturally possess an Entheon’s Affinity simply because they live alongside or train Entheon of that Affinity. Human interaction with Affinity is primarily technological, academic and practical rather than equivalent to an Entheon’s innate Affinity. Whether humans can naturally manifest Affinity themselves, and the extent to which this is possible, remains a subject of study rather than an assumed universal human ability.

7.4 Affinity in the Environment

Affinity is not restricted to individual Entheon. Certain environments naturally contain stronger concentrations or manifestations of particular Affinities. This contributes to the distribution of Entheon species throughout the world and helps explain why particular species are more commonly encountered in some regions than others. Examples include:

  • Volt activity around electrical infrastructure and naturally charged environments
  • Tide phenomena around oceans, lakes, rivers and other bodies of water
  • Flame phenomena in volcanic or geothermally active environments
  • Frost phenomena in cold northern environments
  • Verdant phenomena in forests and other biologically rich environments
  • Stone phenomena in mountainous, rocky and subterranean environments
  • Gale phenomena in exposed highlands, coastlines and other areas of strong atmospheric movement
  • Metal phenomena around mineral-rich regions and areas of significant metalworking or industrial activity
  • Mystic phenomena around locations with unusual concentrations of anomalous or psychic energy
  • Radiant phenomena associated with intense light, celestial or spiritually significant environments
  • Umbral phenomena associated with darkness, deep underground environments and other areas where light is naturally limited
  • Wild phenomena associated with untamed ecosystems and primal natural environments
  • Dragon phenomena associated with particularly powerful or unusual concentrations of Affinity energy

These environmental associations do not mean that an Affinity can only exist in its preferred environment. Rare Entheon can occur outside their typical habitats, and unusual environmental conditions can produce unexpected Affinity concentrations. Particularly strong or unusual Affinity phenomena may also occur around ancient sites, shrines, caves and other locations with unusual environmental or historical characteristics.

7.5 Affinity and Society

Affinities have developed cultural associations throughout society. Certain Affinities may be commonly associated with particular professions, activities or cultural traditions. A person working with electrical infrastructure may frequently work alongside Volt Entheon, while explorers in northern regions may commonly partner with Frost Entheon. These associations are practical and cultural rather than deterministic. Society does not universally treat Affinities as indicators of personality or character. An individual Entheon’s behaviour is determined by the individual, not simply by its Affinity. Nevertheless, stereotypes can exist. Some people may associate Umbral with secrecy, Flame with aggression, Mystic with intelligence or Radiant with nobility. Such assumptions are cultural perceptions rather than established truths.

7.6 Affinity Research

Affinity is a significant area of scientific research. Researchers study:

  • how Affinity develops
  • why Entheon possess particular Affinities
  • how dual Affinities interact
  • how Affinity relates to biology
  • how Affinity manifests in the environment
  • how Affinity influences Entheon abilities
  • how Affinity can be measured and detected
  • how Affinity can interact with technology

Much is understood about the practical effects of Affinity, particularly its applications in battling and technology. However, the underlying nature and origin of Affinity remain subjects of ongoing research. Ancient records and unusual Affinity phenomena occasionally provide evidence that challenges current scientific understanding.

7.7 Affinity in Everyday Life

Affinity has applications far beyond battling. Communities make practical use of Affinity-related technology and Entheon abilities in areas such as:

  • energy generation
  • construction
  • agriculture
  • transportation
  • environmental management
  • mining
  • manufacturing
  • communications
  • emergency services
  • entertainment and sport

The extent of Affinity use varies between regions according to local resources, technology and the Entheon species naturally found there. Affinity therefore forms part of ordinary life rather than existing solely as a combat mechanic.

7.8 Affinity and Battle

Affinity is a fundamental component of Entheon combat. An Entheon’s Affinity influences the techniques it can learn, the effectiveness of techniques used against it and the way its natural abilities interact with other Affinities. Using a technique that matches one of an Entheon’s own Affinities provides the Same-Affinity Bonus. For dual-Affinity Entheon, either of their Affinities can provide the bonus when matched by the technique. Affinity effectiveness is determined by the established combat system and includes multiple levels of effectiveness rather than a simple effective/ineffective binary. The complete Affinity interaction chart is maintained as part of the Creature Combat System rather than being duplicated here. Affinity provides the foundation for combat interactions, but it does not determine the outcome of a battle by itself. Trainer strategy, individual Entheon capabilities, abilities, techniques, statistics, positioning and circumstances all remain relevant.

7.9 Affinity Does Not Define the Individual

Affinity is an important part of an Entheon’s identity, but it is only one part. An Entheon’s Affinity does not determine:

  • personality
  • intelligence
  • morality
  • temperament
  • loyalty
  • Bond with its Trainer
  • preferred behaviour
  • willingness to battle

A Flame Entheon can be gentle. An Umbral Entheon can be affectionate. A Radiant Entheon can be mischievous. A Mystic Entheon can be stubborn. A Wild Entheon can be highly intelligent and disciplined. The Affinity describes what an Entheon is naturally aligned with, not who that individual Entheon is.

8. Technology Level

The world of Entheon is a highly modern, technologically advanced society. Everyday life is broadly comparable to the modern real world, with smartphones, high-speed internet, advanced transportation, modern medicine and sophisticated consumer technology all being commonplace. However, the existence of Entheon and Affinity has pushed technological development beyond the level currently available on Earth. Technology is therefore not merely contemporary. In several fields, the world is noticeably more advanced than modern Earth.

8.1 General Technology

Most people have access to modern consumer technology. Common technologies include:

  • smartphones and personal communication devices
  • high-speed wireless internet
  • computers and tablets
  • digital entertainment
  • online shopping and banking
  • advanced vehicles
  • high-speed rail
  • commercial aviation
  • sophisticated medical equipment
  • artificial intelligence
  • robotics
  • automated infrastructure

Technology is integrated into everyday life and is not considered unusual or futuristic by the people who use it. The presence of Entheon has also created entire industries that have no direct equivalent in present-day society.

8.2 Communication

Communication technology is highly developed. Smartphones or equivalent personal devices provide:

  • voice and video communication
  • messaging
  • internet access
  • navigation
  • photography and recording
  • emergency services
  • digital identification
  • Trainer and Entheon-related applications

High-speed global communication is normal. Social media, streaming services, online communities and other forms of digital communication are established parts of everyday life.

8.3 Transportation

Transportation is highly developed, but does not rely on the same combination of private cars, buses and aircraft common on present-day Earth. The world’s transportation infrastructure has developed around the existence of Entheon, Capture Crystals and Affinity-based technology. Major settlements are connected through sophisticated transportation networks, with different systems used depending on distance, terrain and regional conditions. Common forms of transportation include:

  • High-speed rail and rail-based transit between major settlements
  • Magnetic or other advanced rail systems in densely populated areas
  • Ferries and other watercraft for coastal and island travel
  • Cable and suspended transit systems in mountainous or difficult terrain
  • Affinity-assisted transportation where local Entheon or environmental phenomena make it practical
  • Specialized long-distance transit for travel between distant regions

Personal transportation exists, but private vehicle ownership is not the defining feature of everyday mobility. Urban environments are consequently designed around walkability, public transit and compact communities, with transportation infrastructure integrated into the architecture of the city rather than requiring sprawling road networks. Air travel exists where long-distance travel genuinely requires it, but it is not necessarily the dominant form of interregional transportation. Entheon can also play supporting roles in transportation and logistics, particularly in environments where their natural abilities provide an advantage. However, they are partners and living creatures rather than disposable transportation equipment.

8.4 Capture Crystal Technology

Capture Crystals are one of the world’s most distinctive technologies. They provide a safe and practical method for transporting partnered Entheon without requiring physical containment. Capture Crystal technology is sufficiently mature to be widely available, although different models and levels of quality exist. Crystals are not simply magical storage devices. They are sophisticated pieces of technology that interact with an Entheon’s biological and energetic state. The technology allows an Entheon to enter a compact stored state while maintaining its individual identity and wellbeing. Capture Crystals are therefore an ordinary part of Trainer life, although specialized and higher-quality models may be considerably more expensive than standard versions.

8.5 Entheon-Related Technology

The existence of Entheon has created entire fields of specialized technology. This includes:

  • Entheon identification systems
  • biological scanners
  • Affinity detection equipment
  • battle analysis systems
  • training equipment
  • environmental monitoring systems
  • specialized medical equipment
  • habitat monitoring
  • automated care systems
  • specialized transportation equipment

Trainer technology can provide detailed information about an Entheon’s condition, capabilities and development, although technology does not replace the Trainer’s own understanding of their partner.

8.6 Medicine

Human medicine is highly advanced. Modern hospitals use sophisticated diagnostic equipment, robotics, AI-assisted analysis and advanced surgical technology. Entheon medicine is an equally developed field. Specialists study the biological differences between Entheon species and develop treatments appropriate to their physiology. Affinity research has also contributed to medicine, with certain Affinity-related phenomena and Entheon abilities having legitimate medical applications. However, Entheon abilities do not make conventional medicine obsolete. They are another tool within an advanced medical system.

8.7 Energy and Infrastructure

Modern electrical infrastructure exists alongside technologies that make use of Affinity-related phenomena. Volt, Flame, Tide, Stone, Verdant, Metal and other Affinities can have practical industrial applications. This has allowed certain technologies to develop beyond their equivalents on Earth. Affinity-based systems may contribute to:

  • electricity generation
  • energy storage
  • heating and cooling
  • construction
  • manufacturing
  • environmental management
  • water treatment
  • agriculture
  • transportation

Conventional energy sources and infrastructure still exist. Affinity technology supplements and enhances modern infrastructure rather than completely replacing it.

8.8 Artificial Intelligence and Robotics

Artificial intelligence is highly developed and widely used. AI assists with:

  • research
  • logistics
  • transportation
  • medicine
  • education
  • communications
  • business
  • environmental monitoring
  • industrial automation

Robotics is similarly advanced. Industrial robots are commonplace, while more sophisticated autonomous systems are used in hazardous environments, laboratories, hospitals and specialized industries. AI and robotics remain tools created and operated by humans rather than being treated as equivalent to the intelligence and agency of Entheon.

8.9 Technology and Everyday Life

For ordinary people, advanced technology is simply part of daily life. People can:

  • order products online
  • communicate instantly across continents
  • access entertainment and information anywhere
  • use automated services
  • travel rapidly between regions
  • receive sophisticated medical treatment
  • interact with AI systems
  • monitor their Entheon’s health and development digitally

Kaleo is recognizably modern, but technologically more advanced than present-day Earth. A person walking through a major city should see smartphones, electric vehicles, skyscrapers, digital advertising, autonomous systems and high-speed transportation—not a fantasy village with a few futuristic gadgets awkwardly inserted into it. The major difference is that this technological development occurred in a world where Entheon and Affinity have always existed. That difference has shaped science, engineering, medicine, transportation and everyday life in ways that have no direct equivalent on Earth.

9. Settlements & Regions

The geography of Kaleo is established separately through the regional map and associated regional material. This section establishes the social and practical structure of settlements and travel without duplicating existing geographical descriptions or encounter tables.

9.1 Settlement Hierarchy

Settlements vary considerably in size and importance. Major Cities are the principal population and economic centres of their regions. They generally contain extensive services, administrative institutions, advanced infrastructure and major Trainer facilities. Towns are substantial population centres that serve surrounding communities. They commonly have medical facilities, shops, accommodation, food establishments and other essential services, but have less infrastructure and specialization than major cities. Villages and Small Settlements serve smaller local populations. Their services are more limited and residents often rely on nearby towns or cities for specialized healthcare, advanced equipment and administrative services. Remote Communities exist in areas where geography, climate or local resources make large settlements impractical. These communities may be separated from major population centres by significant wilderness and can have very different lifestyles from urban populations. Specialized settlements may also develop around particularly valuable resources, research facilities, major landmarks or unusual environmental conditions.

9.2 Major Cities

The major cities shown on the established regional map serve as the primary hubs of their respective regions. Each city has its own character, local economy and cultural identity rather than functioning as interchangeable versions of the same urban environment. Major cities generally provide:

  • extensive medical and Entheon-care facilities
  • major transportation connections
  • a wide range of shops and services
  • accommodation
  • schools and educational institutions
  • administrative services
  • Trainer facilities
  • entertainment and cultural venues
  • access to specialized technology

The exact character of each city is established in its individual regional or location entry rather than repeated here.

9.3 Regional Administration

The regions of Kaleo are geographic and cultural divisions as well as practical administrative areas. Regional governments manage local matters while larger governmental institutions coordinate issues that cross regional boundaries. Local authorities are responsible for matters such as:

  • infrastructure
  • public services
  • local regulations
  • environmental management
  • settlement planning
  • public safety
  • regional transportation

The existence of regional government does not prevent considerable cultural differences between regions. Local customs and priorities can therefore vary substantially while still existing within the same broader society.

9.4 Settlement Infrastructure

Infrastructure varies according to settlement size. Major cities provide the broadest range of services, while smaller communities rely increasingly on nearby population centres for specialized needs. Established settlements commonly provide some combination of:

  • medical care
  • Entheon healthcare and recovery
  • food and hospitality
  • general retail
  • Capture Crystal services
  • communication networks
  • public transportation
  • accommodation
  • banking and financial services
  • administrative facilities
  • Trainer-related services

Remote communities may have considerably more limited access to these services. This creates a practical distinction between travelling through populated areas and travelling through wilderness.

9.5 Regional Travel

Kaleo’s established transportation network connects its major settlements through primary routes, secondary trails and maritime connections. The major routes shown on the regional map represent established travel corridors between population centres, while secondary routes connect smaller communities and less-travelled areas. Travel between settlements is generally safe enough for ordinary civilian travel, but conditions become less predictable as travellers move away from established routes. Wilderness travel can involve:

  • difficult terrain
  • changing weather
  • limited communications
  • wild Entheon
  • limited access to medical facilities
  • long distances between settlements

Trainers are therefore generally better prepared for extended travel than ordinary travellers, particularly when deliberately travelling through remote habitats. Lume’s position within the central sea makes maritime transportation particularly important to its connection with the surrounding regions.

9.6 Regional Identity

Geography is only one component of regional identity. Each region develops its own combination of:

  • architecture
  • food
  • occupations
  • local traditions
  • festivals
  • attitudes toward Entheon
  • relationship with technology
  • clothing and aesthetics
  • local customs

These differences are generally matters of culture and history rather than rigid boundaries. People regularly travel between regions, and major cities tend to be culturally diverse as a result.

9.7 Major Landmarks

The established regional map includes several major landmarks that are significant enough to be recognized beyond their immediate surroundings. These include the Great Tree, Great Windmill, Great Lake and the Highreach hot springs. Major landmarks may have geographical, cultural, historical, scientific or recreational significance. Not every landmark needs to have the same type of importance. Some are simply remarkable natural features, while others have acquired significance through their relationship with local communities or Entheon. Individual landmark histories can be expanded in their relevant regional or location entries when they become important to the story.

9.8 Urban and Wild Boundaries

The transition between civilization and wilderness is an important part of Kaleo. Major population centres are highly developed and technologically sophisticated, but large areas between settlements remain comparatively wild. Established routes provide reliable connections between communities, while areas beyond those routes can contain substantially more natural habitat and wild Entheon. This means that travelling through Kaleo can involve moving between highly advanced urban environments and genuinely wild landscapes within relatively short distances. The presence of advanced technology does not mean that nature has been eliminated from the world. Instead, modern civilization and extensive wild habitats coexist throughout Kaleo.

10. Wild Habitats

Wild Entheon exist throughout the natural environments of Kaleo. Their distribution, behaviour and interactions with humans are influenced by their individual characteristics, Affinity, habitat and environmental conditions. The specific Entheon species encountered in each region and habitat are established separately in the regional encounter material.

10.1 Wild Entheon as Part of the Ecosystem

Wild Entheon are a natural part of Kaleo’s ecosystems. They are not simply creatures that appear for Trainers to encounter. They live independently of human society and form populations, territories and ecological relationships within their habitats. Wild Entheon may:

  • forage and hunt
  • establish territories
  • form groups or social structures
  • compete with other species
  • cooperate with other Entheon
  • migrate
  • reproduce
  • rest and shelter
  • interact with their environment

Different species occupy different ecological niches, and individual behaviour can vary considerably even within the same species.

10.2 Habitat and Behaviour

An Entheon’s natural habitat influences its behaviour. Environmental conditions can affect:

  • where an Entheon chooses to live
  • what resources it seeks
  • when it is active
  • how it interacts with other species
  • how territorial it becomes
  • how it responds to humans

Not every Entheon encountered in a particular habitat will behave identically. Personality, Nature and individual experience remain relevant to wild Entheon just as they do to partnered Entheon.

10.3 Daily and Seasonal Activity

Wild Entheon have natural activity patterns. Some species are primarily active during the day, while others are nocturnal or most active around dawn and dusk. Some species may also respond to:

  • weather
  • temperature
  • seasonal changes
  • tides
  • environmental Affinity fluctuations
  • breeding periods
  • availability of food or other resources

Consequently, the same habitat can contain different Entheon populations or encounter possibilities depending on when it is explored.

10.4 Territories and Social Behaviour

Some Entheon are territorial, while others are highly social. A species may live:

  • alone
  • in pairs
  • in family groups
  • in herds or flocks
  • in larger colonies
  • alongside other species

Territorial behaviour also varies between individuals. An Entheon encountered in the wild may ignore a human completely, become curious and approach them, attempt to intimidate them, or become aggressive if it believes its territory, offspring or resources are threatened. Powerful Entheon may establish particularly large territories, but strength alone does not determine territorial behaviour.

10.5 Human Interaction with Wild Entheon

Humans regularly enter wild habitats for travel, research, recreation, resource gathering and Trainer activities. Most encounters do not automatically result in conflict. Wild Entheon have different reactions to humans depending on their species, personality, previous experiences and immediate circumstances. Some are naturally wary and flee. Some are curious and approach. Some tolerate humans at a distance. Others may become aggressive if approached too closely or otherwise threatened. Trainers therefore cannot assume that every wild Entheon will immediately attack simply because it has been encountered.

10.6 Rare and Unusual Encounters

Some Entheon are significantly rarer than others. An uncommon Entheon should feel genuinely uncommon rather than appearing constantly simply because it is listed as an available encounter. Rare encounters may depend on:

  • specific habitats
  • time of day
  • weather
  • environmental conditions
  • season
  • particularly remote locations
  • unusual Affinity concentrations

Some species may therefore be technically present within a region while remaining difficult to encounter. Legendary Entheon are treated separately from ordinary wild populations.

10.7 Human Impact and Conservation

Wild habitats are subject to varying degrees of human influence. Major settlements and developed areas can alter surrounding ecosystems, while remote regions may remain largely untouched. Kaleo maintains conservation efforts to protect important habitats and Entheon populations. These may include:

  • protected natural areas
  • habitat restoration
  • population monitoring
  • restrictions on development
  • research programs
  • controlled access to environmentally sensitive locations

The capture of wild Entheon is not inherently considered harmful, but irresponsible capture practices, habitat destruction and exploitation of wild populations can create ecological problems.

10.8 Habitat Does Not Equal Affinity

Affinity influences where an Entheon may naturally thrive, but habitats are not restricted to a single Affinity. A forest is not exclusively Verdant. A mountain is not exclusively Stone. An ocean is not exclusively Tide. Natural environments can support Entheon with very different Affinities, particularly where multiple ecological niches overlap. This allows wild habitats to contain diverse Entheon communities rather than functioning as simple representations of a single Affinity.

10.9 Wilderness and Exploration

Although Kaleo is technologically advanced, significant areas of wilderness remain. Established settlements and transportation routes provide reliable access between population centres, but travelling beyond developed areas can involve genuine exploration. Remote wilderness may contain:

  • difficult terrain
  • limited communication coverage
  • dangerous weather
  • unfamiliar ecosystems
  • territorial Entheon
  • rare species
  • abandoned structures or ruins
  • unusual Affinity phenomena

Trainers who deliberately explore such areas generally prepare accordingly. The existence of advanced technology does not eliminate the unpredictability of nature. Wild Entheon remain wild.

11. Research Institutions

Research institutions exist throughout Kaleo, ranging from universities and specialist laboratories to field stations and regional Entheon research centres. They study Entheon biology, behaviour, ecology, Affinity, Capture Crystal technology and related fields. Research is not controlled by a single organization or individual. Different institutions specialize in different areas and may have very different facilities, funding and research priorities.

11.1 Regional Entheon Research Centres

Most major regions have at least one prominent Entheon research centre associated with a major settlement. These centres may conduct:

  • Entheon research and observation
  • regional ecological studies
  • Entheon health and welfare research
  • Affinity research
  • monitoring of local Entheon populations
  • conservation programs
  • research into local habitats and environmental phenomena
  • education and outreach for Trainers and the public

Research centres may maintain partnerships with universities, Gyms, conservation organizations, medical institutions and other research organizations.

11.2 Starter Entheon Programs

Regional research centres commonly operate Starter Programs for young people beginning their journey as Trainers. A prominent researcher, professor or senior specialist generally oversees the program. This person is often a respected local authority on Entheon and may become a recognizable figure within the region. People who begin their journey through the centre may remember them for many years. Starter Programs are not simply systems for distributing predetermined Entheon. The researcher considers factors such as:

  • the individual’s circumstances
  • their experience and confidence
  • compatibility with potential Entheon partners
  • the Entheon’s temperament and Nature
  • the needs and welfare of the Entheon

The goal is to establish a healthy partnership between Trainer and Entheon rather than simply providing the most powerful available creature. Starter Entheon may come from species maintained specifically for the program, carefully managed local populations, or Entheon raised at the research centre.

11.3 Regional Researchers

The person overseeing a regional Starter Program does not need to be a world-renowned authority on every aspect of Entheon research. They are generally a respected local expert with particular knowledge of the Entheon, ecosystems or Affinities of their region. They may have:

  • an academic background
  • years of field research
  • experience working with wild Entheon
  • expertise in a particular Affinity or habitat
  • connections to universities and research organizations
  • experience working directly with Trainers and local communities

Their personalities and research philosophies can vary considerably. Some are enthusiastic and eccentric. Some are serious academics. Some are adventurous field researchers. Some are primarily concerned with conservation. Regional researchers therefore have their own identities and areas of expertise rather than functioning as identical representatives of a single global institution.

11.4 Research and Trainers

Researchers frequently work with Trainers. Trainers can provide valuable field observations that researchers cannot easily obtain in laboratories, while researchers can provide Trainers with information about Entheon biology, habitats and Affinity. Some researchers are themselves experienced Trainers. Field researchers may maintain their own Entheon partners for:

  • fieldwork
  • protection
  • environmental research
  • sample collection
  • assisting with wild Entheon
  • accessing difficult environments

Research and Training are therefore closely connected aspects of society rather than completely separate fields.

11.5 Research Ethics

Modern Entheon research recognizes that Entheon are sentient beings with individual agency. Research involving living Entheon is therefore subject to welfare and ethical standards. Particular care is taken with:

  • invasive procedures
  • long-term confinement
  • experimentation
  • wild Entheon populations
  • breeding programs
  • Capture Crystal research

Research involving a partnered Entheon generally requires the cooperation of its Trainer while also considering the Entheon’s own welfare and agency. Illegal or unethical experimentation can occur, but it is not considered normal or acceptable scientific practice.

11.6 Research Facilities

Research institutions can include:

  • universities
  • regional Entheon research centres
  • Affinity laboratories
  • field research stations
  • conservation centres
  • Entheon medical institutes
  • Capture Crystal laboratories
  • ecological monitoring facilities

The scale and specialization of each institution varies according to its location and purpose.

11.7 Research and Society

Research is an ordinary and visible part of modern Kalean society. The public benefits from research through improvements in areas such as:

  • Entheon healthcare
  • human medicine
  • conservation
  • Affinity technology
  • Capture Crystal technology
  • agriculture
  • energy
  • environmental management

Researchers therefore occupy many different roles in society, ranging from university academics and laboratory scientists to field biologists, medical specialists, conservationists and independent researchers.

12. Trainer Organizations & Leagues

Trainer battling is a recognized part of Kalean society, ranging from casual battles and local events to formal Gym challenges and professional competition. The Trainer system provides a framework for registering Trainers, recognizing official Gym challenges, organizing competitions, and maintaining standards for Entheon welfare and battle safety.

12.1 Trainer Registration

Individuals who capture and train Entheon can register as Trainers. Trainer registration provides an official record of the individual’s status as a Trainer and their legally recognized Entheon partnerships. Registration is common and accessible. Many people become Trainers without intending to pursue professional competition or complete the Gym challenge. Registered Trainers may participate in activities such as:

  • Gym challenges
  • organized tournaments
  • local battle events
  • professional competitions
  • recognized Trainer work
  • field research and exploration
  • other activities involving trained Entheon

12.2 Recognized Gyms

Kaleo has seven recognized Gyms. Each Gym:

  • specializes in an established Affinity
  • has a designated Gym Leader
  • maintains an established six-Entheon roster
  • uses an established battle format
  • awards a Gym Crest when successfully defeated
  • can be challenged independently of the other Gyms

The seven recognized Gyms are:

  1. Everhope Gym — Gale
  2. Gullhaven Gym — Tide
  3. Thermalis Gym — Flame/Stone
  4. Harveston Gym — Verdant
  5. Lakecrest Gym — Volt
  6. Northreach Gym — Metal/Frost
  7. Fairhaven Gym — Mystic

Gym Leaders are not simply powerful Trainers. Each Gym has its own philosophy, assessment approach and battle style, with the challenge intended to test more than raw battle strength. Individual Gym Leader profiles establish their personalities, philosophies, battle styles, assessment methods and Entheon rosters.

12.3 Gym Challenges

Gym challenges are formal battles between a challenger and a recognized Gym Leader. The seven Gyms may be challenged in any order. There is an informal recommended progression based on expected difficulty, but this is guidance rather than a mandatory progression system. A sufficiently experienced Trainer may challenge a more difficult Gym without first completing the others. A challenger who defeats a Gym Leader receives that Gym’s Crest.

12.4 Gym Crests

There are seven Gym Crests, corresponding to the seven recognized Gyms. A Crest is a physical achievement and insignia representing a Trainer’s successful completion of a Gym challenge. Crests may be collected in any order. Possessing Gym Crests is recognized as an important achievement among Trainers and may qualify a Trainer for higher levels of organized competition.

12.5 Gym Leader Rosters

Every Gym Leader has an established roster of six Entheon. The roster represents the Leader’s established team, battle philosophy and Affinity specialization. When a Gym’s official battle format requires fewer than six Entheon, the Leader selects the appropriate number from their established roster. Gym Leaders may adapt their selection and battle approach to the challenger without abandoning the identity and philosophy of their Gym.

12.6 Competitive Battling

Trainer battling exists across a broad spectrum. A Trainer may:

  • battle casually with friends
  • participate in local competitions
  • challenge Gyms
  • participate in organized tournaments
  • pursue professional battling

Professional battling can form part or all of a Trainer’s career. Professional Trainers may earn income through competition, sponsorships, appearances, exhibitions and other recognized activities. The existence of professional battling does not make every Trainer a professional competitor. Many Trainers battle primarily for recreation, personal development, exploration, companionship or other reasons.

12.7 Local Battle Events

Battling is also incorporated into local culture and community celebrations. Towns and cities may host:

  • festival battles
  • exhibition matches
  • demonstrations
  • community tournaments
  • charity battles
  • seasonal competitions
  • other organized events

These events may be competitive or primarily recreational and entertaining. Entheon battling is therefore integrated into everyday Kalean society rather than being restricted to Gyms and professional competition.

12.8 The Kalean League

The Kalean League is the broader institution overseeing recognized Gym challenges and major competitive battling. The League establishes standards for recognized Gyms, Gym Leaders, official Gym challenges and major competitive events. Completing all seven Gym challenges is a significant Trainer achievement and provides access to higher levels of organized competition. League-level competition includes organized tournaments and other high-level competitive events. The League does not require Trainers to complete the seven Gyms in a specific order.

12.9 Other Trainer Paths

The Gym and League system represents only one path available to Trainers. Trainers may instead or additionally pursue careers and activities involving:

  • Entheon research
  • conservation
  • field exploration
  • Entheon care
  • breeding
  • performance and entertainment
  • specialized professional work
  • competitive battling
  • casual or recreational training

A person does not need to pursue all seven Gym Crests, or professional competition, to be considered a successful Trainer. The Trainer system therefore supports many different relationships between humans, Entheon and society.

13. Economy & Currency

Kaleo has a modern, technologically advanced economy. Economic life is broadly comparable to that of a developed modern society, while Entheon, Affinity and the technologies surrounding them have created industries and economic activities unique to Kaleo.

13.1 Currency

Kaleo uses a unified national currency known as the Vale. Both physical and digital forms of Vale are used. Digital payments are widespread due to Kaleo’s advanced communications and financial infrastructure, while physical currency remains available and accepted for ordinary transactions. The Vale is used for everyday purchases, wages, services, travel, Trainer expenses and larger commercial transactions.

13.2 General Economy

Kaleo has a diverse economy spanning traditional industries and sectors created or expanded by the existence of Entheon and Affinity. Major economic sectors include:

  • technology
  • manufacturing
  • healthcare
  • Entheon healthcare
  • agriculture
  • research
  • energy
  • transportation
  • construction
  • entertainment
  • tourism
  • retail
  • finance
  • conservation
  • professional battling and related industries

Different regions have different economic strengths based on their geography, resources, infrastructure and local industries.

13.3 Employment

Most people work in ordinary civilian professions. Entheon ownership does not automatically make someone a professional Trainer, and many people who own Entheon have conventional careers. Common professions include:

  • teachers
  • doctors and medical professionals
  • researchers
  • engineers
  • scientists
  • farmers
  • technicians
  • tradespeople
  • business owners
  • retailers
  • hospitality workers
  • artists and entertainers
  • government employees
  • conservationists
  • transportation workers

Training Entheon can also form part or all of a person’s profession. Professional Trainers may earn income through competitions, sponsorships, exhibitions, instruction and other Trainer-related activities.

13.4 Trainer Economics

Being a Trainer involves ongoing expenses. Common Trainer expenses include:

  • food and supplies
  • accommodation
  • travel
  • Entheon healthcare
  • Capture Crystals
  • training equipment
  • battle equipment
  • clothing and personal equipment
  • Entheon-specific food and care products

Trainers can fund their journeys in different ways. Some have personal savings or family support. Others work while travelling, receive payment for Trainer-related services, participate in competitions or earn money through battles and other activities. Professional Trainers may also receive sponsorships or other forms of commercial support. A Trainer does not need to be wealthy to begin travelling, although maintaining a large and highly equipped team can become expensive.

13.5 Entheon-Related Economy

Entheon are an important part of the Kalean economy. Entire industries exist around:

  • Capture Crystal production
  • Entheon healthcare
  • Entheon food and nutrition
  • training equipment
  • breeding
  • research
  • conservation
  • transportation
  • specialized technology
  • Affinity-related technology

Entheon-related products and services range from inexpensive everyday necessities to highly specialized and expensive equipment.

13.6 Affinity and Industry

Affinity has influenced the development of numerous technologies and industries. Affinity-related applications can contribute to:

  • energy production and storage
  • manufacturing
  • construction
  • agriculture
  • environmental management
  • medicine
  • transportation
  • specialized industrial processes

The use of Affinity in industry does not eliminate conventional technology. Instead, the two have developed alongside one another.

13.7 Regional Economies

The regions of Kaleo have different economic identities. Geography, climate, natural resources, infrastructure, local industries and Affinity-related opportunities all influence regional economies. Coastal regions may place greater emphasis on maritime industries and fishing. Agricultural regions may have extensive farming and food production. Regions with significant mineral resources may develop strong mining and manufacturing industries. Major cities generally function as economic hubs connecting multiple industries and regions. Regional economic differences contribute to the distinct identities of Kaleo’s communities.

13.8 Wealth and Accessibility

Kaleo has meaningful differences in wealth and income. Advanced technology is widespread, but access to the most sophisticated or specialized equipment is not necessarily equal. Basic communication technology, healthcare, transportation and everyday consumer goods are broadly accessible. High-end technology, specialized medical treatment, premium Capture Crystals, rare Entheon-related equipment and other luxury goods can be considerably more expensive. Becoming a Trainer is nevertheless accessible to people from a wide range of economic backgrounds. A Trainer’s financial circumstances may influence how they travel, what equipment they can afford and what opportunities are available to them, but wealth does not determine whether someone can form meaningful partnerships with Entheon.

13.9 Trainer Rewards and Battle Income

Battling can provide legitimate income, but not every battle is financially rewarded. Official competitions may offer:

  • prize money
  • sponsorship opportunities
  • professional recognition
  • advancement within competitive circuits

Gym challenges are primarily assessments rather than a simple method of earning money. Local competitions and exhibitions may offer smaller prizes or other forms of compensation. Professional battling can therefore become a viable career, but it is not a guaranteed source of wealth.

14. Shops & Services

Kaleo has a broad network of shops and services supporting everyday life, Entheon ownership and Trainer activities. The availability and specialization of services varies according to the size and importance of a settlement. Major cities provide the widest range of options, while smaller communities generally focus on essential goods and services.

14.1 General Retail

Ordinary retail is a normal part of life throughout Kaleo. Depending on the size of the settlement, people may have access to:

  • supermarkets and grocery stores
  • clothing shops
  • electronics retailers
  • household goods
  • pharmacies and personal-care stores
  • specialty shops
  • convenience stores
  • restaurants and food vendors

Major cities contain large commercial districts and specialized retailers, while smaller settlements generally provide a more limited selection.

14.2 Entheon Supplies

Specialized shops provide products for Entheon care and training. Common products include:

  • Entheon food
  • treats and supplements
  • grooming products
  • toys and enrichment equipment
  • training equipment
  • protective equipment
  • battle supplies
  • Capture Crystals
  • Entheon accessories

Products vary considerably in quality and price. Basic supplies are widely accessible, while specialized training equipment and premium products can be considerably more expensive.

14.3 Medical and Entheon Healthcare

Healthcare is widely available throughout Kaleo. Human medical facilities range from small local clinics to large hospitals and specialist medical centres. Entheon healthcare operates alongside human medicine and includes:

  • routine examinations
  • treatment of injuries
  • post-battle recovery
  • preventative care
  • nutritional guidance
  • rehabilitation
  • specialist treatment
  • emergency care
